49     /// </summary>

50     public string OrderByClause { get; set; }

51  }

    Don't be overwhelmed by these properties. You will most likely use two properties - SqlWhereClause
    and SqlMetaWhereClause. They are extremely flexible and powerful, but can also be confusing
    at times. Simply put, SqlWhereClause can be used when you want to filter on CatalogEntry or
    CatalogNode table, while SqlMetaWhereClause can be used when you you want to filter on the
    metafields.

    To understand how they can be used, let's try an example: find entries which are going to expire
    today. We know that the expire date is represented by EndDate in CatalogEntry table, so we need to
    have a filter on SqlWhereClause:

                        Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 11: Search  208

1 CatalogSearchOptions options = new CatalogSearchOptions();
2 CatalogSearchParameters searchParams = new CatalogSearchParameters();
3 searchParams.SqlWhereClause = " [CatalogEntry].[EndDate] < " + DateTime.UtcNow.ToStr\
4 ing("yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss");
5 int totalCount = 0;
6 CatalogEntryDto entryDto = catalogSystem.FindItemsDto(searchParams, options, ref tot\
7 alCount);

    Simple, right? You can certainly extend this to find entries which are going to expire next week or
    next month.

                     It's worth noting that when you work with database, you should always use UTC timezone.
                     All DateTime values are supposed to be saved as UTC in Commerce database.

    Let's take another example - let's say we want to find the entries belong to a specific metaclass.
    With the CatalogSearchOptions.Classes we can filter Product, Variant, Package or Bundle, but
    we might want to go further and filter for FashionSKU or SLR. Again, SqlWhereClause can solve this
    because CatalogEntry has a column named MetaClassId which indicates the metaclass of the entry.

1 //Assuming we don't know the Id, but only the name of the MetaClass
2 MetaClass metaClass = MetaClass.Load(CatalogContext.MetaDataContext, "DSLR");
3 CatalogSearchOptions options = new CatalogSearchOptions();
4 CatalogSearchParameters searchParams = new CatalogSearchParameters();
5 searchParams.SqlWhereClause = " [CatalogEntry].[MetaClassId] = " + metaClass.Id.ToSt\
6 ring();
7 int totalCount = 0;
8 CatalogEntryDto entryDto = catalogSystem.FindItemsDto(searchParams, options, ref tot\
9 alCount);

    Of course, you can combine those condition in one query - you can find entries belong to "DSLR"
    metaclass AND expire next week.

                     You can't, however, search for entries belong to "DSLR" metaclass OR expire next week in
                     one query. You would need to search for both and then combine the result.

While SqlWhereClause is quite straightforward, SqlMetaWhereClause can be a bit tricky to work
with. The idea is still the same - to filter by SQL. If you recall from how metaclasses are stored in the
first chapter, you can see that the tables for metaclasses are dropped in Commerce 9, and replaced
by the views. The views act pretty much the same way, but due to their structure, the query will be
a bit different.

Let's go through another example: find entries which "Color" property, with type LongString has
value of "Blue"

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 11: Search  209

1 MetaClass metaClass = MetaClass.Load(CatalogContext.MetaDataContext, "SLR");
2 CatalogSearchOptions options = new CatalogSearchOptions();
3 CatalogSearchParameters searchParams = new CatalogSearchParameters();
4 searchParams.SqlMetaWhereClause = " META.MetaFieldName = 'Color' AND META.LongString\
5 = 'Blue'";
6 int totalCount = 0;
7 CatalogEntryDto entryDto = catalogSystem.FindItemsDto(searchParams, options, ref tot\
8 alCount);

    So you need to specify field name, by `META.MetaFieldName', and field value, by `META.<field
    type>'.

                     The "new" META.MetaFieldName pattern is introduced in Commerce 9. If you forgot, the way
                     catalog data is stored was significantly changed in Commerce 9. For most parts, that was
                     for the better. However, when it comes to search for entries, it is a drawback. Previously you
                     can search for entries which matches two or more conditions, for example, by using this
                     query META.Color = 'RED' AND META.Size = 'Large'. However, this will not possible in
                     the new pattern. You can only search for entries which match one condition in one query.
                     If you want to filter by both, you would need two queries and then intersect the result. This
                     was acknowledged as a known issue, but will not be fixed, because fixing it would mean
                     introducing another breaking change.

SqlMetaWhereClause and SqlWhereClause are not exclusive - they can be used in conjunction to
create really powerful queries which combine from various condition. You can use the previous
example to find entries which belongs to T-shirt metaclass, and have red color.

                 It's important to remember that Search API:s do not know about, or work with versioning.
                 They can only work with the published data (which are represented by CatalogEntry,
                 CatalogNode and the views represent content properties). Currently there are no API:s to
                 find data from draft versions, and there probably will be no such features.

Find Commerce

Episerver Find, in term of product, is a .NET API:s for ElasticSearch, in term of service, it's
an indexing service built on ElasticSearch. After acquiring Truffler in 2012, Episerver has been
integrating Episerver Find to their core products, allowing contents to be indexed and searched
in a very flexible and powerful way. However, Commerce was lagging behind in this matter,
its integration package, Find Commerce, was not as fully featured as Find CMS. Luckily, Find
Commerce has been giving a lot of attention recently, and with Find Commerce 9.5 [^foo5113],
it's now more or less on par with what Find CMS has to offer.

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 11: Search                    210

Find Commerce vs FindSearchProvider:

1 `FindSearchProvider` is just a wrapper to allow entries to be indexed and searched b\
2 y Episerver Find. It does not leverage the true power of Find, or content in a "prop\
3 er way". The advantage of `FindSearchProvider` is it can be used for older versions \
4 of Commerce, including R1 SP2 and R3, and it can be used in Commerce Manager. In ter\
5 ms of features and flexibility, `FindSearchProvider` can't not compete to Find Comme\
6 rce, it's not even close. If you are developing a new site, and intend to use Episer\
7 ver Find, then Find Commerce should be your first choice. FindCommerce can only work\
8 on front-end site, however this still makes it a great solution because that's wher\
9 e you have to deal with the most complicated scenarios and demanding loads.

                     Find Commerce comes with a separated nuget package, EPiServer.Commerce.Find, and it is
                     not released in Commerce "bundle". It has its own version and release schedule.

    Find Commerce, as Find itself, relies heavily on extension methods and lambda expressions. Are
    you ready to go?

    Find Commerce configurations

    Find Commerce comes with many configurations, and most of them can be done by code. If we
    are to override some configurions, it's best to do that in an IConfigurableModule, which has
    dependency on FindCommerceInitializationModule. This way, we can be sure to override the
    default configuration and dependency registrations.

1 [InitializableModule]
2 [ModuleDependency(typeof(EPiServer.Find.Commerce.FindCommerceInitializationModule))]
3 public class MyFindCommerceInitializationModule : IInitializableModule
4{
5
6}

    By default, Find Commerce will reindex the content everytime its prices or it inventories are updated.
    Inventories can be updated often (especially if your goods are being sold like hot cakes), it might
    make senses to disable reindexing on inventory updated events. How?
    Find Commerce uses CatalogContentEventListener to handle pricing and inventory events. This
    listener will do a check to see if it should index a content when an inventory event triggers - if the
    content type was configured to index the inventories. So this is how Find Commerce configures that:

                                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 11: Search                                                                211

1 protected virtual void ApplyIStockPlacementConventions()

2{

3   SearchClient.Instance.Conventions.ForInstancesOf<IStockPlacement>()

4   .ExcludeField(c => c.InventoryReference)

5   .IncludeField(x => x.Inventories());

6}

   The "conventions" are built around SearchClient.Instance.Conventions. Here we are saying: for
   contents implement IStockPlacement, exclude InventoryReference, but include Inventories() in
   the index. If we want to exclude Inventories from the index, we need to override it

1 public void Initialize(InitializationEngine context)

2{

3   SearchClient.Instance.Conventions.ForInstancesOf<IStockPlacement>().ExcludeField\

4 (c => c.Inventories());

5}

   because our IInitializableModule runs after FindCommerceInitializationModule, this will over-
   ride the default conventions. Our site no longer indices the contents when an inventory event
   triggers. Basically, we can also override any default convention with this method.

   Now consider another scenario. We want to index the content with the inventory information. But
   to reduce the overload, we only index if the the SKU has less than 10 items in stock (if it is more than
   10, just display something like "10+ in stock"). First, we need to unhook the default event listener in
   CatalogContentEventListener:

1 public void Initialize(InitializationEngine context)

2{

3   _broadcaster = context.Locate.Advanced.GetInstance<CatalogKeyEventBroadcaster>();

4   _listener = context.Locate.Advanced.GetInstance<CatalogContentEventListener>();

5   _broadcaster.InventoryUpdated -= _listener.InventoryUpdated;

6}

   Then we need to register our custom listener. As we talked in Part 4 - Events, the sender contains
   list of InventoryRecords, while InventoryUpdateEventArgs contains list of CatalogKeys, Name of
   the event and a flag for ApplicationHasContentModelTypes. Here we need to check if the remaining
   stock in all warehouses of a item is less than 10 or not.

                           Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 11: Search                                                          212

1 private void InventoryUpdated(object sender, InventoryUpdateEventArgs args)

2{

3     var inventoryRecords = sender as IEnumerable<InventoryRecord>;

4     if (inventoryRecords == null)

5     {

6        return;

7     }

8

9     var inventoryGroups = inventoryRecords.GroupBy(c => c.CatalogEntryCode);

10    foreach (var inventoryGroup in inventoryGroups)

11    {

12       if (inventoryGroup.Sum(c=>c.PurchaseAvailableQuantity) < 10m)

13       {

14          _contentEventIndexer.SavingContent(_referenceConverter.GetContentLink(in\

15 ventoryGroup.Key, CatalogContentType.CatalogEntry));

16       }

17    }

18 }

    _contentEventIndexer is an instance of ContentEventIndexer, the class used for indexing con-
    tents in Find, and that's the simple way to index a content - calling SavingContent with the
    ContentReference of the content to be indexed. Still remember our little buddy ReferenceConverter?

    Search with Find Commerce

    If you have experience with Find already, this should be easy. But if you don't, let's break down a
    normal search:

1 var variationContentSearch = SearchClient

2     .Instance

3     .Search<VariationContent>()

4     .For(searchText)

5     .FilterPriceAvailableForCurrentUser()

6     .FilterForVisitor()

7     .Select(x => new

8     {

9        x.ContentLink,

10       Inventory = ((IStockPlacement)x).Inventories(),

11       Prices = ((IPricing)x).Prices(),

12

13       ImageUrl = x.DefaultImageUrl(),

14       x.DisplayName,

15       Description = x.Description(),

                           Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 11: Search                                          213

16      x.Code

17  })

18  .ApplyBestBets()

19  .Track()

20  .Skip(startIndex)

21  .Take(pageSize)

22  .GetResult();

    "Woo woo, wait a minute" - I can hear you. I can understand, this can look scary at first. But not
    after we go through it one by one:

    · We are searching for VariationContent, so no NodeContentBase, or even ProductContent. Find
      is all about strongly types, so if you want to search for "TV" - and you have a content type for
      that, simply change the type to "TVContent".

    · A matching searchText is expected.

    · We only search for entries which have prices available for current user.

    · We only search for entries which are available for current user. So if an entry is only available
      in "US" Market, but the current user is in "Europe" Market, the entry will be filtered out.

    · We don't want the entire content in the search result, we only want some important fields, the
      ContentLink, prices, inventories, the image, display name, etc. Again, it's about strongly typed
      types - so if your content has a property named "ScreenSize", and it's indexed, it can be used
      here.

    · We are applying the "BestBets" here. It's a feature in Find that allows you to link a specific
      content to a keyword. For example, if you know customers often make a typo searching
      for "ASpecailKeyword", instead of "ASpecialKeyword", you can configure that keyword for
      a content which should be in the search result. Or if you know customers often search for a
      keyword that your products do not contain, but you want to promote one anyway.

                        Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 11: Search  214

                                                    An example of best bets in Find UI

   · We want to track the search. For analytics, of course.
   · We are using paging technique here. Note that Skip and Take here are not LINQ, although they

      look the same. Note that Find query and LINQ expressions can't be used in conjunction.
   · After building the query, we finally get the result. As most sites use Find as a cloud service

      (provided by Episerver itself), it's best to make the traffic as small as possible - so the way Find
      works is to only return what you need.

On your search page, you usually tweak the query above, but the core concept is more or less the
same.

Advanced filters

The strongly typed types properties work a bit differently with "complex" (aka nested) types.
Inventory and Prices are example of nested types, and you can have plenty of filtering scenarios for
them in a Episerver Commerce site. For example, filtering entries which have prices in the current
currency, or have prices less than a specific value. Find Commerce gets you covered - it provides a
lot of filters for IPricing and IStockPlacement.
For example, if we want to filter only variations which have (default) prices less than 100, in current
currency, the above query can be rewritten like this:

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 11: Search                                           215

1 var variationContentSearch = SearchClient

2  .Instance

3  .Search<VariationContent>()

4  .For(searchText)

5  .FilterPriceAvailableForCurrentUser()

6  .FilterForVisitor()

7  .Filter(p=>p.PriceLessThan(100m))

8  ...

   It's good that Find Commerce has better documentation about its filters than Find itself. It would
   still take a while for you to discover all the filters, and when to use what, but believe me, it's worth
   your time and effort.

   This section is not supposed to be your complete reference about Find Commerce, but instead a
   glimpse at it. I hope this crash course made you like Find Commerce - and Find in general. You now
   can explore it powers to make your coding days a joyful experience.

   Searching for orders

    Searching for orders is another essential part of your website. Finding for orders which are ready to
    release, or find a specific order by a tracking number, are two examples that you will frequently do.
    Let's see how we can do it.

    Searching for orders shares many characteristics with searching for entries. They even share a large
    portion of API:s. Order searching API:s are built upon OrderSearchOptions and OrderSearchParameters,
    which inherit from SearchOptions and SearchParameters, respectively, so they have same base
    classes as CatalogSearchOptions and CatalogSearchParameters.

    OrderSearchOptions is almost identical with CatalogSearchOptions. The only different is int the
    Namespace property - it limits the search to `Mediachase.Commerce.Orders'. OrderSearchParameters
    is an even more simple inheritance of SearchParameters: it does not have any new properties on its
    own.

    While searching for entries can be done with ICatalogSystem interface, searching for orders (Cart,
    PaymentPlan and PurchaseOrder) can be done with OrderContext class. This class provides many
    methods to find orders, carts, and payment plans. For example, to find for Cart, we can use one of
    these methods:

1 public Cart[] FindCarts(OrderSearchParameters parameters, OrderSearchOptions options)

2 public Cart[] FindCarts(OrderSearchParameters parameters, OrderSearchOptions options\

3 , out int totalRecords)

    Let's write a simple example of searching for carts - we want to delete the carts which are abandoned,
    customers added something to the carts but then left and forgot about it. There is already a scheduled
    job for this, but let's try to reimplement it to see what happens under the hood:

                        Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 11: Search                                                                                  216

1 int startIndex = 0;

2 int totalRecords = 0;

3 int daysOld = 30;

4 OrderSearchParameters parameters = new OrderSearchParameters();

5 parameters.SqlMetaWhereClause = String.Format("Meta.Modified < GETDATE() - {0}", da\

6 ysOld.ToString());

7 OrderSearchOptions options = new OrderSearchOptions();

8 options.StartingRecord = startIndex;

9 OrderContext.Current.FindCarts(parameters, options, out totalRecords);

10 while (totalRecords > 0)

11 {

12    Cart[] carts = OrderContext.Current.FindCarts(parameters, options, out totalReco\

13 rds);

14    //Delete the carts

15 }

          Just like the example to load entries we had before - we are loading and processing carts
          in batches here. In most of the cases, it's better to load and process entries and orders in
          small chunks: loading one by one is a waste, while loading everything at once can result in
          timeout or out of memory exception.

    It looks similar, right? The only difference with the sample code we had with searching for
    entries is we use different class "ShoppingCart". You might wonder where the "Meta.Modified"
    field comes from. Cart, PaymentPlan and PurchaseOrder are metaclasses extend OrderGroup - if
    you still remember the concepts from Order system chapter. When you want to filter on a field
    specific to one of these class, you have to filter with "Meta.<Fieldname>" on SqlMetaWhereClass
    statement, otherwise you filter on "[OrderGroup].<FieldName>" on SqlWhereClause. Let's look at
    another example where we search for wishlists, where we do filter on the [OrderGroup].Name:

1 int startIndex = 0;

2 int totalRecords = 0;

3 int totalCount = 0;

4 OrderSearchParameters parameters = new OrderSearchParameters();

5 parameters.SqlWhereClause = "[OrderGroup].Name = 'WishList'";

6 OrderSearchOptions options = new OrderSearchOptions();

7 options.Classes.Add("ShoppingCart");

8 options.StartingRecord = startIndex;

9 OrderContext.Current.FindCarts(parameters, options, out totalRecords);

10 while (totalRecords > totalCount)

11 {

12    Cart[] carts = OrderContext.Current.FindCarts(parameters, options, out totalReco\

13 rds);

                             Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 11: Search                                                            217

14    totalCount += 20;
15    //Do something with the wish lists
16 }

    SqlWhereClause is just a normal WHERE clause, where you can combine conditions, such as AND and
    OR to form complex query. In above example, we only filter by Name, but with can combine with
    other conditions, such as MarketId. We also have methods to find PaymentPlans, which are almost
    identical to above methods:

1 public PaymentPlan[] FindPaymentPlans(OrderSearchParameters parameters, OrderSearchO\
2 ptions options)
3 public PaymentPlan[] FindPaymentPlans(OrderSearchParameters parameters, OrderSearchO\
4 ptions options, out int totalRecords)

    For PaymentPlan, you can have some searches for the cylce, for example, this code will search for
    PaymentPlans with the cycle of 2 weeks:

1 int startIndex = 0;

2 int totalRecords = 0;

3 int totalCount = 0;

4 OrderSearchParameters parameters = new OrderSearchParameters();

5 parameters.SqlMetaWhereClause = "[META].PlanCycleMode = 2 AND [META].PlanCycleLength\

6 = 2";

7 OrderSearchOptions options = new OrderSearchOptions();

8 options.Classes.Add("PaymentPlan");

9 options.StartingRecord = startIndex;

10 OrderContext.Current.FindPaymentPlans(parameters, options, out totalRecords);

11 while (totalRecords > totalCount)

12 {

13    PaymentPlan[] paymentPlans = OrderContext.Current.FindPaymentPlans(parameters, o\

14 ptions, out totalRecords);

15    totalCount += 20;

16    //Do something with the payment plans

17 }

    And finally, we have some methods to find the PurchaseOrders:

1 public PurchaseOrder[] FindPurchaseOrders(OrderSearchParameters parameters, OrderSea\
2 rchOptions options, out int totalRecords)
3 public PurchaseOrder[] FindPurchaseOrdersByStatus(params OrderStatus[] statuses)

    Enough with the simple cases - let's try a more complicated case. How's about finding purchase
    orders with was made with lineitems which has a specific value of a custom metafield?

                               Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 11: Search  218

1 OrderSearchParameters parameters = new OrderSearchParameters();
2 parameters.SqlMetaWhereClause = "META.Color = 'Green'";
3 parameters.SqlWhereClause = "OrderGroupId IN (Select ObjectId FROM OrderGroup_Purcha\
4 seOrder)";
5 OrderSearchOptions options = new OrderSearchOptions();
6 options.Classes.Add("LineItemEx");
7 PurchaseOrder[] purchaseOrders = OrderContext.Current.FindPurchaseOrders(parameters,\
8 options);

    This is where the complicated stuffs kick in - to search for a metafield value, you'll need to search in
    LineItemEx by adding it to the Classes of OrderSearchOptions - then you would be able to query by
    SqlMetaWhereClause as normal with META.<FieldName>. The only catch here is that you'll have to
    make sure you're finding PurchaseOrder, not anything else. That's why we need the SqlWhereClause
    to limit the search.

    If you want to search for purchase orders which have a specific lineitem by code - unfortunately
    you can't apply the solution above. The problem is, the code belongs to LineItem, which is a system
    metaclass, while OrderSearchOptions.Classes can only work with user metaclasses (OrderGroup is
    a system metaclass, while PurchaseOrder, ShoppingCart and PaymentPlan are not). Here you will
    need to write a quite complex SQL query:

1 parameters.SqlWhereClause = "OrderGroupId IN (SELECT li.OrderGroupId From LineItem l\
2 i WHERE li.Code = 'Pro-Episerver-Commerce')";

    Truth is, Order-search API:s are really powerful - you can do almost anything to find the orders you
    want - however it's less than pleasant to test if your code is correct. I would suggest you to try the
    simple tool here² to verify your query and test the results almost immediately.

Performance considerations

If you have many orders (or carts), and performance is your concern (it should, I - as most people,
like fast eCommerce sites), then finding orders via SearchAPI has something to consider about.

First of all, not APIs are created equal. Some are faster, and some are inevitably slower. OrderContext.FindPurchaseO
is an example of the latter. It queries on a non-indexed column, and even if you add the index, it
does not help much (The Status column has very little distinctive values, that would make a bad
index). So every time you call this method, it'll (most likely) do an Clustered Index Scan, which is
both expensive, slow, and prone to deadlocks. Be careful when you use this method - don't call it
too much, and don't call it concurrently. You might never need it, but I have seen cases when this
causes big troubles.

Second of all - you are going to add your metafields to your classes - PurchaseOrder, OrderForm,
Shipment, LineItem - and chances are you are going to query by them. For example, you might

   ²http://world.episerver.com/blogs/Quan-Mai/Dates/2014/10/Order-searchmade-easy/

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 11: Search                                          219

add a metafield named PaymentReference to your PurchaseOrder, to track the payment from the
payment provider, and you want to get the order from that PaymentReference. It would make senses
to add a non clustered index for that column, if it makes senses.

                 An index makes senses if the values are highly selective, and you frequently query by it.
                 It's worth noting that an index is not a silver bullet, and adding it is not without drawbacks
                 (Adding overhead to insert and delete, for example), so watch your database usage closely
                 and decide if its advantages overweight the disadvantages.

Normally, you don't add index(es) to default/system metafields - because it might interfere with
the system index(es) (existing or might be added in the future). However, there are exceptions
(as always). One of the cases is the Created/Modified columns in OrderGroup_ShoppingCart, and
OrderGroup_PurchaseOrder. Modified on OrderGroup_ShoppingCart will be used to find the "obso-
lete" carts (so you can remove them and keep your system clean), while Created in OrderGroup_-
PurchaseOrder will be used when you want to list the orders placed today, this week, this month, or
this year. Those columns were considered as candidates for non clustered indices, but it was finally
decided they should be added on a per site basis.

Again, adding an index is trying to solve a problem, so only do that if you really have that problem.
Don't try to solve a problem you don't have.

                    Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Part 6: Promotions - Everybody
loves discounts.

Everyone loves discounts, they create the urge of buying things that we might not actually need.
Businesses runs that way, marketing works that way and a good eCommerce site always comes with
discounts.
However it might not be as good or easy as working with discounts as a developer, as redeeming
them as a buyer.
Chapter 12: The old promotion

system.

At this time of writing, a new promotion system has been released and battle-tested, and trust me
it's much better than the old promotion system in many (if not all) aspects, and it will eventually
replace the old one. (The old one is marked as obsolete and will be removed in a future version.
However, I don't expect every Episerver Commerce site to switch to new system overnight. I would
recommend you to convince your boss to use the new promotion system, but that will take time and
effort. We are talking about the old promotion system for completeness, and so you can understand
why we need a new system.

If you can leave the past behind and just have to work with the new promotion system, you can just
skip this chapter and jump directly to the next chapter.

For Episerver promotion system, there are two concepts you need to know: Campaigns and
Promotions.

Campaign is a simple concept, it's more like of a group of promotions. You can set basic properties
such as the valid time, or the markets it applies, and that's it. It's neither significantly sophisticated
nor important, but you'll still have to create a campaign before creating any promotions.

Promotion is a much more complicated concept. It has many settings you'll need to configure to get
it work.
Chapter 12: The old promotion system.  222

                                        A "simple" promotion created in Commerce Manager

Let's walk through the important parts of a promotion:

   · Type of promotion. There are 3 promotion groups - with a total of 12 builtin promotion types
      - available out of the box: CatalogEntry - which applies on lineitems, Order - which applies
      on the entire promotions, and Shipping - which applies on the shipping cost. The type of
      promotion will affect the condition(s) you can select, and the reward(s) you can apply.

Just like the metafields, once you save the promotion, you can't change its type. If you made a

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 12: The old promotion system.  223

mistake and created a promotion in a type that you don't want to, your only option is to delete that
promotion and create a new one:

                                            Shipping promotions seem to be the favourites

                 However, you will learn soon enough that it is not easy to extend a promotion beyond the
                 builtin types.

   · Promotion Group - this is not editable and depends on the type of promotion. Promotion Group
      takes effect in the next setting, which is ...

   · Combination with other promotions: You can choose to combine with other promotions, or
      exclude with other promotions in same group, or exclude all other promotions. The order of
      exclusion will depend on ...

   · Priority: define the order in which promotions will be applied. Higher priority promotions will
      be applied first. In case of exclusion, if a promotion excludes another one, then only the higher
      one will be applied. ³. For example, if you have a "Global exclusive" promotion with highest
      priority applied, then no other promotions will be applied. However, if it's not the highest
      priority and another promotion has been applied, it'll exclude itself.

   · Coupon code: a code which customers must input to be able to get the promotion - it's either
      promotion code, or coupon code, depends on where you are.

   · Active: it should explain itself. You can turn a promotion on or off by this flag.
   · Purchase Condition and Reward: This is actually an complex combination of settings. Depends

      on the type of promotion, you can select the condition to be "If the order subtotal is equal or
      greater than 1000USD", and the reward to be "Get 10% discount off the whole order". There
      are a very huge amount of possible combinations, so make sure you test the condition before
      making it promotion public. This is the strength and also the weakness of the old promotion
      system.

   ³The combination, priority and exclusion is a complicated subject, which does not work perfectly until very last versions of Episerver

Commerce 9. This, again, is a reason why you always should stay updated.

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 12: The old promotion system.                                          224

       · Redemption limits: This will limit the number of times your customers can redeem the
          pro­mLoitmioint .oTnheenrteiraereprtohmreoetipoons,sible settings:
            ­ Limit on one customer (so only logged in customers are entitled to this promotion),
          Yo­u cliamnict opmerboinrdeearllgtrhoruepe., and the lowest possible setting will take effect.

    As a developer, you usually don't work with old promotion system, at least not directly. In real
    world scenarios, merchandisers are usually the one who create the promotions manually (Which
    is supposedly a good thing, it is not developer friendly to create promotions anyway). Your
    responsibility is to ensure those promotions are applied to the carts correctly.

    Luckily for us it can be quite easy to do such task, we just need to call CartValidate or CartPrepare
    workflows, which has an activity named CalculateDiscounts, which itself will do bunches of stuffs
    before calling to MarketingContext to apply the promotions. There are only two cases you should
    care about it:

       · When a customer apply a coupon code. It can be as simple as this:

1   if (!String.IsNullOrEmpty(couponCode))

2   {

3      MarketingContext.Current.AddCouponToMarketingContext(couponCode);

4   }

    · When you want to get a discounted price for entries, such as when you display products on
      sale. It's not really an interesting or challenging task to be honest, but for the completeness of
      the book, we'll discuss it here: We'll have to rely on PromotionHelper (A lot of legacy classes
      from Mediachase named helper) to do the evaluation of promotion, here is an example:

1   public virtual PromotionContext Evaluate(PromotionFilter filter,

2      PromotionEntriesSet sourceEntriesSet, PromotionEntriesSet targetEntriesSet, \

3 bool checkEntryLevelLimit)

4   {

5      var helper = new PromotionHelper();

6      helper.PromotionContext.TargetGroup = PromotionGroup.GetPromotionGroup(Promo\

7 tionGroup.PromotionGroupKey.Entry).Key;

8      helper.PromotionContext.SourceEntriesSet = sourceEntriesSet;

9      helper.PromotionContext.TargetEntriesSet = targetEntriesSet;

10     helper.Eval(filter, checkEntryLevelLimit);

11     return helper.PromotionContext;

12  }

    PromotionFilter is not really a filter of promotions. It's used by MarketingContext to see if it should
    check some additional condition (perhaps it could have been named ValidationConditions). For our
    purpose, we'll have it like this:

                                           Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 12: The old promotion system.                                          225

1 var filter = new PromotionFilter

2     {

3         IgnoreConditions = false,

4         IgnorePolicy = false,

5         IgnoreSegments = false,

6         IncludeCoupons = false

7     };

      · IgnoreConditions is false because we'll actually run the condition to verify if there are
        promotions can be applied.

      · IgnorePolicy is false so any Policy expressions will be validated (however, this feature is rarely
        used).

      · IgnoreSegments is false to validate segment and market. If the current cart does not belong to
        one of the markets the campaign is targeting, then it should not be entitled to get the promotions
        in that campaign.

      · IncludeCoupons is false so MarketingContext will skip validating any coupons.

    The next thing is PromotionEntriesSet. There are two things to fill in: the sourceEntriesSet and
    targetEntriesSet - they are collection of PromotionEntry. While sourceEntriesSet are the entries
    that might match the condition (required by the promotion), then targetEntriesSet are set of entries
    that might receive the promotion. In this case, we're looking for promotion that might apply for
    one entry, then both sourceEntriesSet and targetEntriesSet should be the same. But that's not
    always true. In other kinds of promotion, it can be different. For example, for Order level promotion,
    sourceEntriesSet will be null (because we'll examine order's attribute, such as SubTotal or so),
    while targetEntriesSet are the entire lineitem in the order.

1 private PromotionEntry CreatePromotionEntry(EntryContentBase entry, IPriceValue pric\

2 e)

3{

4     var catalogNodes = string.Empty;

5     var catalogs = string.Empty;

6     foreach (var node in _catalogSystem.GetCatalogRelationDto(entry.ContentReference\

7 .ID))

8     {

9         var entryCatalogName = _catalogSystem.GetCatalogDto(node.CatalogId).Catalog[\

10 0].Name;

11        catalogs = string.IsNullOrEmpty(catalogs) ? entryCatalogName : catalogs + ";\

12 " + entryCatalogName;

13        catalogNodes = string.IsNullOrEmpty(catalogNodes) ? node.Code : catalogNodes\

14 + ";" + node.Code;

15    }

16    var promotionEntry = new PromotionEntry(catalogs, catalogNodes, entry.Code, pric\

                                           Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 12: The old promotion system.                                                                  226

17 e.UnitPrice.Amount);

18    return promotionEntry;

19 }

              I'll leave it here as an exercise - convert this method to use IContentRepository entirely.

    Now we have all the information we need, let' try to get this discount:

1 var sourceSet = new PromotionEntriesSet();
2 sourceSet.Entries.Add(promotionEntry);
3 var promotionContext = Evaluate(filter, sourceSet, sourceSet, false);

    Evaluate will return a PromotionContext, and then we'll have to calculate the discount we get from
    that:

1 private decimal GetDiscountAmount(PromotionContext promotionContext)

2{

3     decimal discountAmount = 0;

4     foreach (var record in promotionContext.PromotionResult.PromotionRecords)

5     {

6          var reward = record.PromotionReward;

7          if (reward.RewardType != PromotionRewardType.EachAffectedEntry

8             && reward.RewardType != PromotionRewardType.AllAffectedEntries)

9          {

10            continue;

11         }

12         if (reward.AmountType == PromotionRewardAmountType.Percentage)

13         {

14            discountAmount = record.AffectedEntriesSet.TotalCost * reward.AmountOff \

15 / 100;

16         }

17         else

18         {

19            discountAmount += reward.AmountOff;

20         }

21    }

22    return discountAmount;

23 }

    The PromotionResult we get from PromotionContext might contain multiple records - each record
    for one promotion which was successfully applied. We only care about the reward of type

                                           Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 12: The old promotion system.  227

PromotionRewardType.EachAffectedEntry or PromotionRewardType.AllAffectedEntries for the
discounted price of the entry. Sum all the reward and we'll have the discountAmount of the entry
with applied promotions.

The code above is to display discounted prices for the product listing page. We don't have to do
extra thing to apply promotions, because it's included in certain workflows such as CartValidate
- so when a customer adds items into cart, we'll call the certain workflows (most commonly,
CartValidate itself) and the promotions will be applied automatically.

                 The old promotion system is one of the reason you'll have to stick with workflows - even if
                 you don't want other stuffs such as inventories checking and adjusting. From what I heard
                 not many developers are fans of Workflows, but it's the way it is. Technically, you can
                 extract the code from certain activities, such as CalculateDiscountsActivity and put them
                 in a helper class which can be called directly from your code. However, it's a tedious work
                 and you would want to avoid it anyway. Also that would create a diversion between your
                 site and Commerce Manager on how promotions are applied, and that you'd want to avoid.

   Coupon

    The old promotion system also supports coupon. As we see above, each promotion can have only
    one coupon, but there is nothing prevents you from having multiple promotions with coupons, and
    an end user can technically applies all of those coupons to get all of those promotions. (Except too
    much discount is potentially bad for your business).
    When a customer add a coupon code, you have to make sure the promotion system knows about it,
    and the promotions which can be redeemed by that coupon are evaluated and - if other conditions
    satisfy - applied (Again there is nothing prevents you from having multiple promotions with same
    coupon). To do such task, we need to add it to the MarketingContext before the promotion system
    is called:

1 MarketingContext.Current.AddCouponToMarketingContext(couponCode);

    The coupon is now stored in MarketingProfileContext. When CalculateDiscounts activity is
    called, MarketingContext will try to get the coupon from MarketingProfileContext and apply the
    promotion.

                     Here is one of the places where the old and new promotion system work on entirely different
                     concept. In the old promotion system, coupon is part of context. In the new promotion
                     system, coupon is part of the cart. It's hard to argue what is better, conceptually. But when
                     it comes to implementations, I'd give my vote to the new promotion system, as explained
                     below.

    Simple, right? but it's not the entire story. MarketingProfileContext is stored in local thread storage
    if there is no HttpContext available, or in HttpContext.Current.Items if there is. So for most of the

                                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 12: The old promotion system.                                          228

    cases, it will be stored in HttpContext.Current.Items. The problem is HttpContext.Current.Items
    is per-request storage. If your customer wants to add a new item into the cart, so if he/she simply
    refreshs the cart page, or changes the address (which in most of the case will send new request to
    server) ­ then the storage ­ and therefore the coupon, is lost. The customer will have to input it
    again, or, you will have to store it somewhere.

    There are two obvious options: Sessions and Cookies. Sessions are more convenient to use, as, in the
    end, it's only a NameValueCollection where you can simply add and get back anything you'd like.
    However, Sessions can't work in a load balancing environment. If you want to use load balancer,
    then Cookies are the only (simple) choice. Here's an example of what you can do in your Cart page:

1 private void ApplyCoupons()

2{

3     var cookie = _cookieService.Get(CouponKey);

4     if (cookie != null)

5     {

6        var coupons = JsonConvert.DeserializeObject<HashSet<string>>(cookie);

7        if (coupons != null && coupons.Any())

8        {

9           foreach (var coupon in coupons)

10          {

11             MarketingContext.Current.AddCouponToMarketingContext(coupon);

12          }

13       }

14    }

15 }

    _cookieService is only simple service for getting the cookie from HttpContext.Current.Request.
    The reason of using HashSet<string> is to allow customers to input multiple coupon codes, but
    they must be unique. And to avoid problem with any special characters in coupon codes, here we
    use JsonConvert.DeserializeObject to form a string from the coupons. If you are certain that your
    coupons will not contain some special characters, a String.Join would be enough . And then you
    need to add the coupon when customer adds it to the cart:

1 var cookie = _cookieService.Get(CouponKey);
2 var coupons = cookie != null ? JsonConvert.DeserializeObject<HashSet<string>>(cookie\
3 ) : new HashSet<string>();
4 coupons.Add(couponCode);
5 _cookieService.Set(CouponKey, JsonConvert.SerializeObject(coupons));

    It's a bit tricky here ­ normally you would only want to save the valid coupons, so you might need to
    check first ­ by adding the coupon to the MarketingProfileContext and run the certain workflows
    to see if it's applied:

                                           Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 12: The old promotion system.                                          229

1 MarketingContext.Current.AddCouponToMarketingContext(couponCode);

2 _cartService.RunWorkflow(OrderGroupWorkflowManager.CartValidateWorkflowName);

3 _cartService.SaveCart();

4

5 if (!GetAppliedDiscountsWithCode().Where(d => couponCode.Equals(d.DiscountCode)).Any\

6 ())

7{

8      return new EmptyResult();

9}

   GetAppliedDiscountsWithCode is a method where you loop over OrderForms, Shipments, LineItems
   to see if their Discounts contain any code. And finally, remove it when the customer no longer
   wants it in the cart (either they actively delete it, or when they placed the order):

1 var cookie = _cookieService.Get(CouponKey);

2 if (cookie != null)

3{

4      var coupons = JsonConvert.DeserializeObject<HashSet<string>>(cookie);

5      if (coupons != null && coupons.Contains(couponCode))

6      {

7         coupons.Remove(couponCode);

8         _cookieService.Set(CouponKey, JsonConvert.SerializeObject(coupons));

9      }

10 }

   And finally remove it when customer placed the order. Remember, just removing the cookie is not
   enough ­ you have to make it expired.

   Redemption

   As we mentioned earlier, you can configured the redemption limits for a promotion. A re-
   demption is "counted" when it's actually used. When certain workflows are called, an activity
   (RecordPromotionUsageActivity) will be invoked to record one usage of the promotion. However,
   this does not necessary count toward the redemption limit, because a `recorded' usage might still be
   "Expired" or "Cancelled". It takes a little more work to record the PromotionUsage when a customer
   checkouts an order:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 12: The old promotion system.  230

1 Dictionary<string, object> dic = new Dictionary<string, object>();
2 dic.Add("UsageStatus", PromotionUsageStatus.Used);
3
4 OrderGroupWorkflowManager.RunWorkflow(Cart, OrderGroupWorkflowManager.CartCheckOutWo\
5 rkflowName, true, dic);

    This will run CartCheckout workflow with an additional context - to record any PromotionUsage
    for the order with Used status.

                                                  Only then the redemption is recorded

                 Now a word of honesty. The redemption handling in old promotion system works terribly
                 in concurrency situation. It fails consistently if two orders are placed at the same time, using
                 same promotion (The only upside of this is the "consistently" part. I'm yet to find any thing
                 good about "inconsistency"). So if you are having a hot promotion which is being used a lot,
                 better avoid setting redemption for it. Or even better, use the new promotion system!

How promotion works?

As complicated and flexbile it is, you might still want to look into how it works. You can of course
treat how the promotions are applied as blackbox, but if that does not satisfies you, then here we go.
The promotion you created, will be turned into a CodeDom Expression. This, along with other
information, will be stored in Promotion table in database. If you want to see how the the Expression
of your promotion looks like, you can go to Marketing/Expressions/Promotion and open it there. No,
I don't like it and it does not really informative or interesting in anyway, but I can cheat two pages
of writing, so here it is:
...

                 In the end it looks terrible in the book, so sorry to disappoint you. It's "only" 188 lines so I
                 can send it to you if you really want it. Mind you, it's only a "simple" condition.

A CodeDom Expression is basically an XML which defines which properties to look at and which
conditions you want to compare. To my eyes personally it is very hard to understand, and even
harder to debug.

                 Mediachase actually released an tool named ExpressionEditor which allows you to edit a
                 promotion expression, to some extend.

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 12: The old promotion system.                                          231

When you run certain workflows which contain the CalculateDiscounts activity, such as CartValidate,
the cart will be pre-processed by MarketingContext. MarketingContext will do certain checks, such
as if the redemption limit has reached or not. If all other conditions passed, MarketingContext will
call the expression validator (by default implementation, is built around the RuleEngine class in
System.Workflow.Activities.Rules) to validate the conditions to see if the promotion's conditions
can be redeemed.

If the cart satisfies all the conditions, then the promotion will be applied - new discounts will be
added to OrderForm.Discounts, Shipment.Discounts, or LineItem.Discounts, depends on the type
of promotion, or new gift item will be added to cart. The promotion usages - which are used to
calculate the redemptions, are also recorded.

It might not sound all that bad about the engine, right? But actually, it is

· It is slow. The strength of the rule-based validation is its flexibility, you can define almost any
  condition/reward you like (it's just not easy to write, and to verify), but its weakness is it's
  slow to validate. The validation of condition is based on RuleEngine will struggle to handle
  high volume of orders, when you have anything more than a few dozens of active promotions
  (I saw customers with 500 promotions or more, and they were being held back because of the
  performance). For the records, the new promotion system is supposed to be able to handle
  10.000 promotions with 1000 active with relative ease.

· It's not developer friendly. The Expression is simply hard to read, write, debug, and impossible
  to test. You, in most case, can only rely on manual testing to verify the correctness of the
  conditions (and I believe you do not like this - automated tests are better in our developers
  perspective.)

· It handles concurrency badly. You can try by adding a redemption limit to a promotion, and
  try to place two orders at the same time. It will fail no matter how simple your condition is
  (it's the redemption limit which is bad at concurrency handling, not the condition itself).

· It lacks of supports for advanced scenarios. For example, there is no way to know if a promotion
  is almost fulfilled to encourage customer to spend more (Spend 100$ or more and get free
  shipping, and they have 90$ in cart).

The old promotion system have been there for a long time, and it works to some extends. However,
Episerver's partner developers never seem to like it, it has been in the high spot of pain-points lists.
The architecture and design old the old promotion system allows very few improvements, both in
term of customization and performance. To be fair it might be the best solution, considered the
human resources and deadline at the time it was written, but it's no longer the way a promotion
system should be. That's why new one was born.

                                       Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion
system

In Episerver Commerce 9.19 released in early July, 2016, the new promotion system is considered
stable enough. The BETA tags are removed. The API:s will still be evolved, new features, new built-in
promotion types will still be added over time, but changing or removing API:s will now requires at
least a major release. The new promotion system will be enabled by default in new installation. There
is no reason for not using the new promotion system now.

                                   Marketing UI, the new editing UI for new promotion system

There are two terms being used by the new promotion system: discount is the term in user-facing
texts, and promotion is for API:s level. In this chapter, we'll use promotion term.
The new promotion system is a completely rewrite of the old one. It is written from scratch. Nothing
is transferred from the old system, even the data. If there are anything "shared", they are concepts
(campaign, promotion, redemption limit) and behaviors.

                 It's unlike both renovations of catalog and order system. In Commerce 9, we only changed
                 the catalog data structure, while the (content) API:s largely remained the same. Or in new
                 "Order abstraction API", the default implementation of new API:s are built on top of old,
                 legacy APIs that work on existing data. (This is, however, not true in case of serializable
                 carts).

Not only that, the new and old promotion exclude each other. There is a flag (which we will talk
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          233

about soon enough) that can be used to turn on each of them. You can choose to run your promotions
in new or old mode, but not at the same time.

                 There was a discussion on supporting both systems at the same time, that would help to
                 reduce the work of partners when they move to new promotion. However, the idea was
                 scrapped as it can be to complicated to get right. I personally think it's a good decision to
                 have only one system "active" at a time.

    The new promotion system has been developed with two things in mind:

        · Performance, it has to be fast.
        · Usability, it must be easy to work with, especially from developer perspective - easy to write

          code, easy to write test, easy to override some default behaviors.

    We'll see if Episerver delivers their promises.
    Before you start, there are a couple of things you need to prepare for your site. Note that if you are
    using Episerver Commerce 9.19 or greater, all of these actions are not required anymore - they are
    included by default. As always, you are highly recommended to use latest version of Commerce.

        · To turn on the new promotion system, it's the same as the new order system. You can either use
          IFeatureSwitch to turn it on, or update the setting in ecf.app.config, as documented here³¹

    From 9.19, new sites created with Episerver Commerce will have the new promotion system enabled
    by default. If you access the Marketing view in Commerce Manager, this warning will be displayed:
    "Episerver Commerce is configured to use new promotion system. Use Marketing UI to manage
    campaigns and promotions instead."
    The existing sites which are using old promotion system will not be affected - as the setting is not
    changed during upgrade. However, if you want to use the old promotion system for newly created
    sites (I don't see any reasons that you should, but of course, you can), remove this configuration
    from Configs\ecf.app.config:

1 <Features>
2 <add feature="WorkflowsVNext" state="Enabled" type="Mediachase.Commerce.Core.Feature\
3 s.WorkflowsVNext, Mediachase.Commerce" />
4 </Features>

         ³¹https://world.episerver.com/documentation/developer-guides/commerce/configuration/feature-switch2/

                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                           234

Starting from Commerce 11, the default value of WorkflowsVNext has been flipped. Previous
it defaults to "Disabled", if you want to use it, you need to turn it on. Now it is defaulted
to "Enabled", and if you don't want to use it, you need to turn it off. If you want to use
the old promotion system in your new site (I don't know why), you will have to explicitly
set it to Disabled. Strictly speaking, the new promotion engine does not rely on the setting
to be activated. You can call it directly - and there are new APIs that make it even easier
to go. The setting is only used for so the ActivityFlowRunner engine will know it should
call new ActivityFlow(s) which use the new promotion engine. You might not need to use
workflows anymore in your frontend site, but they are still used in Commerce Manager

Everything is content (again)

When Episerver designed the new promotion system, one of the first question need to be answered:
How to work with data, including persisting and editing. Several options were considered, in
the end, the content way won. There are several benefits of doing so: the unified API:s (read,
IContentRepository) can be used, and the editing UI can be extended quite easily.

                 Dojo might not be the best JavaScript framework out there, but it is still miles better than
                 the user controls and XML you have in Commerce Manager.

In new promotion system, everything is content - and specifically, CMS content.

                 That comes with two things you might be interested in: when you need to do something
                 with promotion data in lowest level (you probably shouldn't, but you can), you should look
                 into CMS database, not Commerce database. And you can now export your promotions data
                 to import to another site. Yes, now it is possible!

Most of the concepts were carried from the old system, but adapted to the content system. The
structure of promotion contents is more or less similar with other content system system - a root,
campaigns and promotions. A new CampaignRoot was introduced. It's not a virtual content like the
RootContent in Commerce, but is saved to the database eventually. You can always access it by the
static property SalesCampaignFolder.CampaignRoot.
Working with the new promotion means working with two strongly content types - SalesCampaign
and PromotionData, they both reside in EPiServer.Commerce.Marketing namespace and inherit from
BasicContent. The structure of promotion contents is much simpler than the structure of catalog
contents: campaigns are direct children of the Campaign root, and promotions are direct children of
campaigns. There is no other level, and there are no direct relations between campaign-campaign
or promotion-promotion.

                 Strictly speaking, there are indirect relations between promotion and promotion, as we'll
                 find out later.

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                        235

Let's try out the content way to see how it works:

1 var contentRepository = ServiceLocator.Current.GetInstance<IContentRepository>();
2 var campaign = contentRepository.GetDefault<SalesCampaign>(SalesCampaignFolder.Campa\
3 ignRoot);
4 campaign.Name = "Spring2016";
5 campaign.ValidFrom = DateTime.Now;
6 campaign.ValidUntil = DateTime.Now.AddMonths(1);
7 campaign.IsActive = true;
8 contentRepository.Save(campaign, DataAccess.SaveAction.Publish, EPiServer.Security.A\
9 ccessLevel.NoAccess);

    I hope you can see the benefits of unified content API:s - you can read and understand the code
    without any explanation.

I need to remind you again, ServiceLocator is a bad practice, some even go further and say
it's an anti-pattern. I would not recommend it. The only reason you are seeing now because
I want to show you it's IContentRepository. You should be using constructor injection
whenever possible.

As usual, we get an instance of IContentRepository, then create a default SalesCampaign instance
as children of SalesCampaignFolder.CampaignRoot (this is always required, as we learned that
SalesCampaign must be direct children of the CampaignRoot), set some properties, and save it again.

                                                  We created a campaign by code. Yay!

SalesCampaign, PromotionData and their derivatives are handled by the default ContentProvider,
so theoretically, they can have versions. However, the Marketing UI does not support managing
versions (You can't add any gadget other than Catalog one). This might change in the future, but for
now, you should always use SaveAction.Publish with promotion contents.

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                          236

         Another thing that promotion contents are different from normal CMS contents is that it
         does not support localization, at least, not yet.

    Let's take a deeper look at SalesCampaign:

2{

Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          237

      public virtual decimal RevenueGoal { get; set; }

Unlike the old system, while Campaign is only a place holder for promotions, the SalesCampaign
has actually information that can be useful for campaign managers:

1 - `Code`: can be used for tracking, such as Google Analytics tracking code.
2 - `VisitorGroups`: can be used to define groups of users which are entitled to this \
3 campaign.
4 - `TargetMarket`: Only orders in this market are entitled to the promotions in this \
5 campaign. Empty market means no orders can be redeemed, while set it to `EPiServer.B\
6 usiness.Commerce.Constants.AllMarkets` ("EPI_AllMarkets") means all markets.
7 - `RevenueGoal`: This is more of report feature than actually being used in the syst\
8 em.

                 In Commerce 12.4, TargetMarket will be obsoleted. It will be replaced by TargetMarkets
                 - well, you might guess it, it means now you can select multiple markets instead of just
                 one. Previously if you want to have same campaign in Sweden, Norway, and Denmark, you
                 would have to duplicate the campaign and set them to each market. Now you just need one!
                 Isn't that good?

Of course, you can extend SalesCampaign to add the properties you want to. However, in real
world case scenarios, I don't think you, as a developer, will work directly with SalesCampaign
everyday. It should be the work of editors/campaign managers to edit the campaign in the UI. It's
the PromotionData, the processors, and the promotion engine we need to care about.

The promotion engine:

The promotion engine can be considered as the core of new promotion engine. It's a very simple
interface, with only two methods:

                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                     238

1 public interface IPromotionEngine

2{

3     /// <summary>

4     /// Checks the condition for all active promotions, and gives awards for the pro\

5 motions where the condition has been fulfilled.

6     /// </summary>

7     IEnumerable<RewardDescription> Run(IOrderGroup orderGroup);

8

9     /// <summary>

10    /// Checks the condition for all active promotions, and gives awards for the ///\

11 promotions where the condition has been fulfilled. Promotions with higher /// prior\

12 ity (lower Priority value) will be evaluated first and all excluded /// promotions w\

13 ill not be evaluated.

14    /// </summary>

15    IEnumerable<RewardDescription> Run(IOrderGroup orderGroup, PromotionEngineSettin\

16 gs settings);

17 }

    For your convenience, PromotionEngine - the default implementation will do those steps:

      · Loads all promotions available, then filters inactive one and sort them by priority.
      · Filters out promotions which require coupon codes, but there was none supplied with the order

        group.
      · For each promotion:

           ­ Checks for redemption limits of promotion to see if a promotion can be fulfilled.

           ­ Loads the processor which can process the promotion, Evaluate it.

           ­ Skip processing promotion which was excluded by previously applied promotions. (We
             will talk more about exclusion later)

           ­ Each promotion will return a list of Redemption. Applies the redemptions until the
             redemption limits are reached.

      · Return list of RewardDescription. Each RewardDescription contains these information:

1     public class RewardDescription

2     {

3        /// <summary>

4        /// Gets or sets the reward typed, can be percentage, amount or free.

5        /// </summary>

6        public RewardType RewardType { get; private set; }

7

8        /// <summary>

9        /// Gets the discount amount when the discount type is money.

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                            239

10     /// </summary>

11     public decimal UnitDiscount { get; private set; }

12

13     /// <summary>

14     /// Gets the discount amount when the discount type is percentage.

15     /// </summary>

16     public decimal Percentage { get; private set; }

17

18     /// <summary>

19     /// Gets the amount of money this reward has given the order.

20     /// </summary>

21     public decimal SavedAmount { get; internal set; }

22

23     /// <summary>

24     /// Gets the reward typed, can be percentage, amount or free.

25     /// </summary>

26     public RewardType RewardType { get; private set; }

27

28     /// <summary>

29     /// Gets the status.

30     /// </summary>

31     public FulfillmentStatus Status { get; private set; }

32

33     /// <summary>

34     /// Gets the redemptions.

35     /// </summary>

36     public IEnumerable<RedemptionDescription> Redemptions { get; private set; }

37

38     /// <summary>

39     /// Gets the promotion.

40     /// </summary>

41     public PromotionData Promotion { get; private set; }

42

43     /// <summary>

44     /// Gets or sets a description of the promotion result, only for debugging a\

45 nd logging.

46     /// </summary>

47     public string Description { get; set; }

48  }

    Of course, you can implement the IPromotionEngine interface yourself, if you want to. But in most
    of the cases, you can just use the default one. As developers, the things we will mostly deal with is
    the Promotion types and the processors themselves.

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          240

    Promotion type

    New promotion system means strongly typed type all the way. That does mean we define our
    promotions by POCO classes, inherits from PromotionData:

    Note that we don't have to inherit from PromotionData directly, we can inherit from its subclasses
    - EntryPromotion, OrderPromotion and ShippingPromotion, which basically PromotionData with
    DiscountType set (DiscountType.LineItem, DiscountType.Order and DiscountType.Shipping, re-
    spectively). There are three thing interesting about PromotionData we might care about:

      · PromotionSchedule: allow you to set if you want to stick with the campaign schedule, or to
        have its own schedule.PromotionSchedule is a rather simple BlockData:

1     [ContentType(GUID = "C350562B-F36F-4A6E-BDEA-C30D4009280E", AvailableInEditMode \

2 = false)]

      · CouponData: By default, Coupon only contains one simple string field, where you can set the
        coupon code for the entire promotion - more or less the same with the old system. We'll discuss
        later about extending this to support more advanced scenarios.

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system  242

· RedemptionLimitsData: It can be used to limit how many times a promotion can be redeemed.
  You can set it to be per-promotion, per-orderform, or per-customer (Requires customers to be
  logged in, if a promotion has per-customer limit set, it will not be available for anonymous/not
  logged in customers). If multiple limits are applied, then the lowest available value will be used.
  Like PromotionSchedule, it's just a BlockData with three nullable int for the limits.

Promotion data and processor

As we learned above, there are three basic types of promotions: EntryPromotion, OrderPromotion,
and ShippingPromotion.
Each has different characteristics, so we'll examine them one by one.

Order-level promotion

An order-level promotion is a promotion when the conditions, or the rewards, are based on the
criteria of the promotion.

             At this time of writing, Episerver Commerce comes with 2 order-level promotion out of the box

If a customer spend equal or more than a specific amount, he or she will get a 10% discount off the
cart. We will one to support multiple currencies setting, for example, if the current cart is in USD,
we want the threshold to be $100, while if it is SEK, then the threshold is SEK1000, etc...Because this
cart affects the whole order (both the conditions and rewards) then it should be an OrderPromotion.
First, let's create a content type for our promotion:

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          243

1 [ContentType(GUID = "7660BDC2-6F65-4D22-8FDB-812EE7F4074D")]

2 public class SpendAmountGetDiscount : OrderPromotion

3{

4     /// <summary>

5     /// The amount of currencies required to get this promotion.

6     /// </summary>

7     public virtual IList<Money> Amounts { get; set; }

8

9     /// <summary>

10    /// The percent of amount off the order.

11    /// </summary>

12    public virtual decimal Discount { get; set; }

13 }

    The content type, as usual, has to be an unique GUID. The interesting here is Amounts property -
    which is an IList<Money>. This is one of two specially treated IList<T> properties in the Marketing
    UI - the other one is IList<ContentReference>. Build your project and then go to marketing view,
    you now can see our new promotion type, and creating it will give us something like this:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system  244

                                                     A list of money amount to set to

You can see that IList<Money> has been rendered to a very nice "control" (or widget, more precisely).
You can edit the value for each currency separatedly. Easy, right? We did almost nothing and there
are already results! However, there are two things to note: - The list of currencies available depends
on the market you choose for your campaign. By default, "All" option is selected, which mean all
currencies from all markets will be available. Selecting a specific market will limit the currencies
you can set. - All the texts in new promotion is only the type name, which is not really nice. You
can make it better by adding this localization file (for English, you can add files for other languages
in same structure) - as I said, it's just another content type:

10 iscount.</formdescription>

    But not that's the only one visual improvement you can make to your promotion, you can also add
    an "icon/thumbnail" to it:

1 [ContentType(GUID = "7660BDC2-6F65-4D22-8FDB-812EE7F4074D")]
2 [ImageUrl("Images/SpendAmountGetDiscount.png")]
3 public class SpendAmountGetDiscount : OrderPromotion

    But something is not ... right. The other builtin promotions are grouped nicely in three groups: Order
    Promotions, Shipping Promotions and Entry Promotions. Our new custom promotion is standing
    alone. Can we make it into Order Promotions group and place it on top of the list? Of course.

1 [ContentType(GUID = "7660BDC2-6F65-4D22-8FDB-812EE7F4074D", GroupName = "orderpromot\
2 ion", Order = 100)]
3 public class SpendAmountGetDiscount : OrderPromotion

    Having GroupName set to "orderpromotion" will put our promotion into Order Promotion groups -
    while Order will determine how should it be positioned with other promotions - lower Order value
    means higher position in the list.
    The only thing remaining is to create a processor, which will be called by the promotion engine to ap-
    ply the promotion. We'll have to inherit from PromotionProcessorBase<T>, where T is a class of type

                                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          247

    PromotionData. In this case, it's our SpendAmountGetDiscount promotion. At runtime, promotion
    engine will do a little magic (read, refection), to get the processor which can process a specific pro-
    motion - so it will look up for a class inherits from PromotionProcessorBase<TSpecificPromotion>
    to process a TSpecificPromotion promotion.

1 public class SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor : OrderPromotionProcessorBase<SpendAmou\

2 ntGetDiscount>

3{

4     /// <summary>

5     /// Evaluates a promotion against an order form.

6     /// </summary>
