8 PromotionProcessorContext context)

9     {

10       throw new NotImplementedException();

11    }

12

13    protected override bool CanBeFulfilled(SpendAmountGetDiscount promotionData, Pro\

14 motionProcessorContext context)

15    {

16       throw new NotImplementedException();

17    }

18

19    protected override PromotionItems GetPromotionItems(SpendAmountGetDiscount promo\

20 tionData)

21    {

22       throw new NotImplementedException();

23    }

24 }

                     The processors can be reused. That means, if you have a class SpendAmountGetDiscountEx
                     which inherits from SpendAmountGetDiscount and adds some extra properties for data (but
                     they are not used in calculation), you don't have to create a processor for it - you can just
                     reuse SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor - PromotionEngine can handle it just fine.

    There is a method we have to implement - Evaluate will take a PromotionData which contains the
    conditions and rewards of the promotion, and the a context (where we can get information about
    other data such as the OrderGroup itself). This PromotionProcessorContext contains almost any
    information from the "context" we need to evaluate the promotion:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          248

1 public class PromotionProcessorContext

2{

3     /// <summary>

4     /// Gets the <see cref="IOrderGroup"/> that is being evaluated.

5     /// </summary>

6     public IOrderGroup OrderGroup { get; }

7

8     /// <summary>

9     /// Gets the current order form that the promotion engine is processing at the t\

10 ime.

11    /// </summary>

12    public IOrderForm OrderForm { get; }

13

14    /// <summary>

15    /// Gets the current order form sub total that the promotion engine is processin\

16 g at the time. This is calculated before every promotion has been evaluated.

17    /// </summary>

18    public decimal CurrentOrderFormSubTotal { get; }

19

20    /// <summary>

21    /// Gets the price matrix for entries.

22    /// </summary>

23    public OrderFormPriceMatrix EntryPrices { get; }

24

25    /// <summary>

26    /// Gets the price matrix for shipments.

27    /// </summary>

28    public ShipmentPriceMatrix ShipmentPrices { get; }

29 }

    For our processor, we will create a helper method to calcuate the order subtotal - where we also need
    to care about the discounts applied by previous promotions. Lucky for us, we can use an existing
    extension method IOrderForm.GetSubTotal(Currency) to calculate the subtotal of the order form.

    And then Evaluate is simple enough. RewardDescription provides a static method to create an
    instance with percentage discount:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          249

1 protected override RewardDescription Evaluate(SpendAmountGetDiscount promotionData, \

2 PromotionProcessorContext context)

3{

4     var orderForm = context.OrderForm;

5     var threshold = promotionData.Amounts.FirstOrDefault(c => c.Currency == context.\

6 OrderGroup.Currency);

7     var subTotal = orderForm.GetSubTotal(context.OrderGroup.Currency).Amount;

8     if (threshold.Amount <= subTotal)

9     {

10       var redemption = new List<RedemptionDescription> { CreateRedemptionDescripti\

11 on(orderForm) };

12       return RewardDescription.CreatePercentageReward(FulfillmentStatus.Fulfilled,\

13 redemption, promotionData, promotionData.Discount, "The promotion has been fulfille\

14 d");

15    }

16    else

17    {

18       return RewardDescription.CreateNotFulfilledDescription(promotionData, Fulfil\

19 lmentStatus.NotFulfilled);

20    }

21 }

    The next thing to implement is CanBeFulfilled. This method serves as a quick check to see if we
    should further process the promotion or not. As Evaluate can be "slow" to execute, and in such cases
    we should avoid running it as much as possible. In this example, our check in CanBeFulfilled is
    simple - because we defined a list of Money to apply this promotion, so we will check for the currency
    of the order being evaluated:

1 protected override bool CanBeFulfilled(SpendAmountGetDiscount promotionData, Promoti\

2 onProcessorContext context)

3{

4     return promotionData.Amounts.Any(c => c.Currency == context.OrderGroup.Currency);

5}

    The final thing is GetPromotionItems. It should return an PromotionItems instance, which in-
    dicates the items which should satisfy the conditions, and the items which should be in the
    rewards. PromotionsItems has a constructor which takes 3 parameters: a PromotionData instance, a
    CatalogItemSelection for conditions and a CatalogItemSelection as reward.

    As we don't really care about the items in the cart, and the reward is for the entire order, so here's
    what it should look like:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                           250

1 protected override PromotionItems GetPromotionItems(SpendAmountGetShippingDiscount p\

2 romotionData)

3{

4   return

5   new PromotionItems(

6           promotionData,

7           new CatalogItemSelection(null, CatalogItemSelectionType.All, true),

8           new CatalogItemSelection(null, CatalogItemSelectionType.All, true));

9}

   We will take more about CatalogItemSelection in upcoming section.

   If the SubTotal of the orderForm is greater than or equal the required amount, then we give the
   discount. To let the promotion engine knows about that, we return a list of RedemptionDescription,
   which each contains the information of what has been given discount for (the entire order, specific
   line items, or the shipping cost). Here we are using a protected method (CreateRedemptionDescription)
   from the parent class OrderPromotionProcessorBase, to create a RedemptionDescription contains
   information about our OrderForm. In case the amount does not satisfy the requirement, we simply
   return a NotFulfilled RewardDescription.

   Let's try to see if it works - Add a promotion with the required spent amount of USD1500 and the
   discount amount of 10%, then try to add something to the cart, of course, with the "WorkflowsVNext"
   flag enabled.

                                                                   Ta da! It works

                    I got a tweet from a reader saying that this picture is not correct - "Your price" should
                    be 1000$. That is a reasonable question, however this is indeed correct. Because this is an
                    order level discount, the price for each item is not changed, however the discount will be
                    "distributed" for each item, which is 100$ in this case.

   One interesting thing in above code is the RedemptionDescription - the processor is supposed to
   return that "description" to the promotion engine about the affected entities, the saved amount etc.

                                                    Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          251

    In this case, we have a promotion which affect the entire order, so we return an AffectedOrder object
    which points to the orderForm. In later examples, we will talk about other types of promotion which
    takes lineitem or shipment, and they will have different affected entities.

    We kept it simple for demonstration, but let's consider a more advanced scenario: If you set the
    target to be USD1500, and the customer already has spent USD1200 in the cart, it's really nice to
    display some message like "Spend $300 more and get a 10% discount". In the end, we need to boost
    our sales while still make our customers happy, a little push like this can be more effective than you
    would think (I can speak for myself in this matter!)

    To do that, let's add a property to SpendAmountGetDiscount promotion (and of course you might
    want to add a localized string for this new property):

1 public virtual decimal AlmostFulfilledThreshold { get; set; }

    This property allows editors to set the threshold that customers start seeing the notification to "spend
    more". Now we can update Evaluate method to handle this number:

1 if (threshold.Amount * promotionData.AlmostFulfilledThreshold > subTotal)

2{

3     return RewardDescription.CreateNotFulfilledDescription(promotionData, Fulfillmen\

4 tStatus.NotFulfilled);

5}

6 else

7{

8     var redemption = new List<RedemptionDescription> { CreateRedemptionDescription(o\

9 rderForm) };

10    if (threshold.Amount < subTotal )

11    {

12       return RewardDescription.CreatePercentageReward(FulfillmentStatus.Fulfilled,\

13 redemption, promotionData, promotionData.Discount, "The promotion has been fulfille\

14 d");

15    }

16    else

17    {

18       return RewardDescription.CreatePercentageReward(FulfillmentStatus.PartiallyF\

19 ulfilled, redemption, promotionData, promotionData.Discount, "The promotion is almos\

20 t fulfilled");

21    }

22 }

    You now might wonder about which statuses you can use for RewardRedemption, here's the full list
    of FulfillmentStatus:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          252

1 [Flags]

2 public enum FulfillmentStatus

3{

4     NotFulfilled = 1 << 0,

5

6     PartiallyFulfilled = 1 << 1,

7

8     Fulfilled = 1 << 2,

9

10    CouponCodeRequired = 1 << 8,

11    Excluded = 1 << 9,

12    VisitorGroupRequired = 1 << 10,

13    RedemptionLimitReached = 1 << 11,

14

15    InvalidCoupon = NotFulfilled | CouponCodeRequired,

16    InvalidCombination = NotFulfilled | Excluded,

17    MissingVisitorGroup = NotFulfilled | VisitorGroupRequired,

18    NoRedemptionRemaining = NotFulfilled | RedemptionLimitReached,

19 }

    Normally, you will only return one of first three statuses: NotFulfilled, PartiallyFulfilled or
    Fulfilled from your processor. Other statuses are expected to be returned by the implementation
    of IPromotionEngine. (Of course, nothing prevent you from implementing it your own and use those
    statuses as well).

    Now we created a promotion which can return a PartiallyFulfilled RewardDescription in case
    the customer is close to their reward. Of course, as the seller, you want to let them know about that
    - to encourage them to spend some more money to get rewarded. You should be able to check it
    when a customer adds a new item to the cart - a quick evaluation for promotions, but nothing is
    applied. That's when the other overload of IPromotionEngine.Run comes in to play - you can pass
    a PromotionEngineSettings which specifies the statuses you are interested in, and if you want to
    apply the reward or not. Here's the code in the "Mini Cart" can look like:

1 var promotionEngine = ServiceLocation.ServiceLocator.Current.GetInstance<IPromotionE\
2 ngine>();
3 var rewards = promotionEngine.Run(cart, new PromotionEngineSettings() { RequestedSta\
4 tuses = RequestFulfillmentStatus.PartiallyFulfilled, ApplyReward = false });
5 var almostFulfilledPromotions = rewards.Where(r => r.Status == FulfillmentStatus.Par\
6 tiallyFulfilled);

    You now have a list of almost fulfilled promotions - and it's up to you to display some encouragement
    to the customers. They will be happy spending more money and you will be happy selling more
    items. It's a win-win situation!

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                     253

       In this section we talked about IPromotionEngine.Run, so you can get
       used to the API:s. However, in real code, you can use some of the
       extensions method, most notably IOrderGroup.ApplyDiscounts() and
       IOrderGroup.ApplyDiscounts(PromotionEngineSettings). In above example, it can
       be rewritten to cart.ApplyDiscounts(new PromotionEngineSettings() ...). There is
       another overload which take an IPromotionEngine as parameter, so you can mock it for
       unit-testing.

    Unit testing

    As I mentioned earlier, one of the reasons for the existing of new promotion system, is to make it
    easier to write tests. With the old system, the only possible way for automation test is to write
    integration tests: Set up a running environment mimicking a working site, then try to create a
    campaign, a promotion (which is, tedious) , then create a cart and add line items to it, and call
    the workflows to get the promotion applied, and verify the result. Trust me, you won't like it.

    The approach in new promotion system allows you to easily write unit test for your processor. It
    is much more easier to mock your class up and verify your logic only. While you should write
    integration tests nonetheless, you should prefer to write unit tests - they are easier to understand,
    and fast to run. You can use any unit testing framework of choice, but in this example we will use
    xUnit, one of the most popular options (which is also used by Episerver Commerce team internally).

    From the code of Evaluate, it looks like we should verify three cases: When the promotion
    is not fulfilled, when it is almost fulfilled and when it's fulfilled. We will need to construct
    an instance of SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor and run Evaluate on an setup instance of
    PromotionProcessorContext which contains an IOrderForm which might or might not satisfies the
    condition. Then we assert the return value.

    There was a small problem with our code - Evaluate use IOrderForm.GetSubTotal(Currency) -
    which does not really "mockable" (Extension methods are not really mockable in general, right?). We
    need to change it to the other overload IOrderForm.GetSubTotal(Currency, IOrderFormCalculator),
    where we can mock IOrderFormCalculator, so our new processor will look like this:

1 public class SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor : OrderPromotionProcessorBase<SpendAmou\

2 ntGetDiscount>

3{

4   private IOrderFormCalculator _orderFormCalculator;

5

6   public SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor(IOrderFormCalculator orderFormCalculator)

7   {

8      _orderFormCalculator = orderFormCalculator;

9   }

10

11  protected override RewardDescription Evaluate(SpendAmountGetDiscount promotionDa\

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          254

12 ta, PromotionProcessorContext context)

13    {

14       var orderForm = context.OrderForm;

15       var threshold = promotionData.Amounts.FirstOrDefault(c => c.Currency == cont\

16 ext.OrderGroup.Currency);

17       var subTotal = orderForm.GetSubTotal(context.OrderGroup.Currency, _orderForm\

18 Calculator).Amount;

19       ...

20

21    }

22 }

    This is a perfect example of dependency injection - we inject the "dependency" into the constructor.
    Now let's create some tests. Creating an instance of SpendAmountGetDiscount is easy enough
    - it's just another POCO object. We need to create an instance of IOrderGroup to mock the
    Currency, and then an instance of IOrderFormCalculator to mock for GetSubTotal. We test
    for SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor and the result it would return when the subtotal of the
    OrderForm fulfills or not fulfills the requirement, so we mock the value returned by GetSubTotal.

    We are using Moq here, but of course you can use any mock framework of your choice - the syntax
    can be slightly different, but you get the idea:

1 public class SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessorTest

2{

4     PromotionProcessorContext _processorContext;

5

11            Discount = 10m,

13       };

14       var orderGroupMock = new Mock<IOrderGroup>();

15       orderGroupMock.Setup(o => o.Currency).Returns(Currency.USD);

16       _processorContext = new PromotionProcessorContext(orderGroupMock.Object, new\

17 FakeOrderForm(), null, null, null);

18    }

19

21    {

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                255

22         var orderFormCalculatorMock = new Mock<IOrderFormCalculator>();

23         orderFormCalculatorMock.Setup(c => c.GetSubTotal(It.IsAny<IOrderForm>(), It.\

24 IsAny<Currency>())).Returns(new Money(subTotal, Currency.USD));

25

26         return new SpendAmountGetDiscountProcessor(orderFormCalculatorMock.Object);

27    }

28 }

    And there are the tests we wanted to write

    These tests have a pretty good test coverage. You can still test for the CanBeFulfilled method where
    the currency of the IOrderGroup does not match one of the currencies in Amounts. I'll leave that for
    you!
    Redemption limit
    We learned about the RedemptionLimits property of PromotionData to limit how many times can
    a promotion be applied. They are usually set manually by the editors - it's an easy job to do - but
    there are cases when you don't want it to be set - but fixed. For example, in case of an order-level
    promotion, it does make senses to allow only 1 per order. And we don't want editors to change that
    (by accident). How?
    Promotion system has an attribute PromotionSettings which has a property FixedRedemptionsPerOrder:

1 [PromotionSettings(FixedRedemptionsPerOrder = 1)]
2 [ContentType(GUID = "7660BDC2-6F65-4D22-8FDB-812EE7F4074D")]
3 [ImageUrl("Images/SpendAmountGetDiscount.png")]
4 public class SpendAmountGetDiscount : OrderPromotion

                                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          257

                                            And the per-order redemption limit is now disabled

   Many builtin promotions have this setting already, including any types derived from OrderPromotion.

   Item Promotion

   Now we create our first promotion with the new promotion system, I hope that you liked it - or at
   least found it easy enough. But we should not stop there. As an expression which is common said in
   Sweden: "Practices make perfect" (I'm sure it's said everywhere, but a little more here), we should
   try to create another one to make sure we fully understand the system. Let's say we want to give a
   10% discount on any items in a specific category. This is a builtin promotion in Episerver Commerce
   ("Buy From Category Get Discount") - but we'll implement ourselves to see how it works.

                    I don't want this. I want to have some new, interesting stuffs. However Episerver Commerce
                    already contains all most common promotions. And I want to avoid too complicated
                    promotions. As long as you understand the principles, you can create any promotions you
                    like.

   As usual, we need to define a promotion type:

2 ion", Order = 12345)]

To specify the category we want to give this discount, we need a ContentReference property. This
has a lot of attributes decorated:

      · AllowedTypes means this can only accept NodeContent, (and it derived classes). You won't be

        able to drag and drop other catalog content types to this property in On Page Editing mode.
      · This property belongs to the "Condition" region of the promotion.
      · You can only select a catalog node/category for this property.

    In Order promotion we talked about GetPromotionItems - however for order promotion it does not
    really matter. For this entry promotion, GetPromotionItems is important because it limits the number
    of entries we need to check.

1 protected override PromotionItems GetPromotionItems(BuyFromCategoryGetItemDiscount p\

2 romotionData)

3{

4     var targetItems = new CatalogItemSelection(new[] { promotionData.Category }, Cat\

5 alogItemSelectionType.Specific, true);

    Here the condition and the reward are pointing to same Category. The final boolean parameter
    of CatalogItemSelection constructor is MatchRecursive, and it being true means if the Category
    contains sub-categories, then their entries will be included as well.

            In this example we only set on category. However it's totally possible to have multiple
            categories, if we change the property to IEnumerable<ContentReference> instead. Try to
            make that change and update GetPromotionItems as well.

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                          260

    Next is the CanBeFulfilled. As we learned in previous promotion type - CanBeFulfilled is a
    shortcut for the promotion engine to know if it should proceed the promotion further, or just skip
    it. In this case, we should check if the Category is set and if the IOrderForm contains any items. I'll
    leave that to you as exercise.

    The final piece is Evaluate. We will have to get all the descendants of the specified Category, get
    their codes, and then compare them to the lineitems in the cart. If one ore more of them match
    those codes, then we will give the discount. It does not sound difficult, but not easy either. Luckily
    for us, Episerver already provides CollectionTargetEvaluator to do exact the same thing. Its only
    public method, GetApplicableCodes, takes the collection of lineitems in the order, the collection of
    ContentReference defined by the promotion, and a flag (boolean) to indicate if it should check that
    collection recursively. (If the collection contains a category, and the flag is set to true, then it will
    look into sub-categories of that as well)

           CollectionTargetEvaluator is highly optimized for performance, it would be amazing if
           you can write even faster code.

1 protected override RewardDescription Evaluate(TEntryPromotion promotionData, Promoti\

2 onProcessorContext context)

3{

4     var lineItems = GetLineItems(context.OrderForm); //GetLineItems is a protected m\

5 ethod of PromotionProcessorBase

6     var target = _targetGetter(promotionData);

7     var affectedCodes = _targetEvaluator.GetApplicableCodes(lineItems, new [] { prom\

8 otionData.Category }, true);

9

10    if (!affectedCodes.Any())

11    {

12         return NotFulfilledRewardDescription(promotionData, context, FulfillmentStat\

13 us.NotFulfilled);

14    }

15

16    ...

17 }

    Now it's tricky part. If a customer buys two items from Category, is it counted as one, or two
    redemptions? What if he buys one item with quantity two? We will assume that both cases are
    counted as 1 redemption, for simplicity.

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          261

1 var affectedLineItems = lineItems.Where(c=> affectedCodes.Contains(c.Code));
2 //CreateRedemptionDescription is a protected method of EntryPromotionProcessorBase
3 var redemption = CreateRedemptionDescription(new AffectedEntries(affectedLineItems.S\
4 elect(c => new PriceEntry(c))));
5 return RewardDescription.CreatePercentageReward(FulfillmentStatus.Fulfilled, new [] \
6 { redemption}, promotionData, 10.0m, "The promotion is fulfilled");

    And we are done!

                     This is of course a simplified version of the builtin promotion. Handling the redemption
                     count can be a complicated scenario when you need to support the decimal quantity. What
                     if there are only 2 redemptions left, and there are 3 items qualified in the cart? That's done by
                     a complex mechanism in the new promotion - involving OrderFormPriceMatrix. We might
                     get to it later ;)

    Shipping promotion

    Now let's work with the final type of promotion - shipping promotion. One of the shipping
    promotion I see the most here in Sweden is "Buy items with at least value of <something>, get
    free shipping" - which we are going to implement now. This is actually an builtin promotion in
    Episerver Commerce, but for educational purposes - let's try to implement that promotion in new
    system.

1 [ContentType(GUID = "87771CF4-2ABE-4DB2-835A-7467575B772B", GroupName = "shippingpro\

2 motion", Order = 12345)]

3 [PromotionSettings(FixedRedemptionsPerOrder = 1)]

4 [ImageUrl("Images/SpendAmountGetFreeShipping.png")]

6{

7     /// <summary>

11    [PromotionRegion(PromotionRegionName.Condition)]

12    public virtual PurchaseAmount Condition { get; set; }

13 }

    You're now familiar with all of these attributes. The only thing new here is the ShippingPromotion
    - the base type for shipping promotions. Unlike OrderPromotion or EntryPromotion, which only
    contain the DiscountType property, ShippingPromotion also contain the list of Guid of shipping
    methods:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          262

1 public abstract class ShippingPromotion : PromotionData

2{

    ShippingMethods are select-able via Marketing UI, so an editor can specify if one shipping method
    (for example, standard shipping) can be free, while customers still have to pay for other ones (for
    example, express shipping), even if the order has fulfilled the condition.

    PurchaseAmount is an out-of-box block which contains the list of money amounts and the PartiallyFulfilledThresh
    (which serves same purpose as AlmostFulfilledThreshold we had).

1 public class PurchaseAmount : ConditionBlock

2{

3     public virtual IList<Money> Amounts { get; set; }

4     //From ConditionBlock

5     public virtual decimal PartiallyFulfilledThreshold { get; set; }

6}

    By default, PartiallyFulfilledThreshold is set to 0.95m, but you can easily set it to another value.
    (At this point of writing, it can be only set by code, and not available to editors in the UI. That's why
    we had our AlmostFulfilledThreshold to see how it works, and editors can easily set that value).

    Now it's the time to implement the processor. Episerver Commerce provides to base processors for
    shipping promotion, ShippingPromotionProcessorBase<T> which inherits from PromotionProcessorBase
    which provides some methods to work with Shipments, and FreeShippingPromotionProcessorBase
    which extends it even further. For educational purposes, we should implement our processor by
    inheriting from PromotionProcessorBase, however due to some limitations (some constructors can't
    be accessed from APIs) - we will need to stick with at least ShippingPromotionProcessorBase.

    The code above shows how it's done internally, but we can of course use the extension method
    provided by Episerver Commerce. It can be rewritten as following:

1 protected override bool CanBeFulfilled(SpendAmountGetFreeShipping promotionData, Pro\

2 motionProcessorContext context)

3{

    Now it's time for more complex methods. As we learned previously, GetFulfillmentStatus should
    return one of three value: NotFulfilled if the promotion is not fulfilled at all. PartiallyFulfilled
    for the case when the AlmostFulfilledThreshold is reached, and Fulfilled when the conditions
    are satisfied. As we only care about the SubTotal of the cart, and we already have CanBeFulfilled
    to filter out the cases when it can't be fulfilled, the method should be easy enough:

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          265

1      protected override FulfillmentStatus GetFulfillmentStatus(SpendAmountGetFreeShip\

2 ping promotionData, PromotionProcessorContext context)

3      {

4         var subTotal = context.CurrentOrderFormSubTotal;

5         if (subTotal >= conditionAmount.Amount)

6         {

7            return FulfillmentStatus.Fulfilled;

8         }

9

10        if (subTotal >= conditionAmount.Amount * promotionData.PurchaseAmount.Partia\

11 llyFulfilledThreshold)

12        {

13           return FulfillmentStatus.PartiallyFulfilled;

14        }

15

16        return FulfillmentStatus.NotFulfilled;

17     }

    Now it's time for the heart of the processor Evaluate method.

1      /// <summary>

2      /// Evaluates the shipping promotion.

3      /// </summary>

4      /// <param name="promotionData">The promotion model.</param>

5      /// <param name="context">The context for the promotion processor evaluation.</p\

6 aram>

7      protected override RewardDescription Evaluate(TShippingPromotion promotionData, \

8 PromotionProcessorContext context)

9      {

10        var fulfillmentStatus = GetFulfillmentStatus(promotionData, context);

11

12        if (!fulfillmentStatus.HasFlag(FulfillmentStatus.Fulfilled))

13        {

14           return RewardDescription.CreateFreeItemReward(

15                     fulfillmentStatus,

16                     Enumerable.Empty<RedemptionDescription>(),

17                     promotionData,

18                     fulfillmentStatus.GetRewardDescriptionText(_localizationService)\

19 );

20        }

21

22        var redemptions = GetRedemptions(promotionData, context.OrderForm);

23

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          266

24         return RewardDescription.CreateFreeItemReward(

25         fulfillmentStatus,

26         redemptions,

27         promotionData,

28         fulfillmentStatus.GetRewardDescriptionText(_localizationService);

29    }

    GetRedemptions is an protected method from ShippingPromotionProcessorBase, and it's the reason
    we had to inherit from it. It returns a list of RedemptionDescription which each of them defines the
    objects which should be affected by the promotion. In our case, it's the shipments being free.

    Coupon codes

    Coupon codes (or promotion codes) are an essential part of promotions. In the previous chapter,
    we learned on how to work with coupons in old promotion system - adding the coupons to
    MarketingContext and how to "persist" them between requests. In the new system, it's much simple
    to add the coupons to the cart:

1 cart.GetFirstForm().CouponCodes.Add(coupon);

    CouponCodes is an ICollection<string> property of IOrderForm. And that's it, this will be carried
    between requests without you have to deal with Session or cookie. Once you save the cart, the
    coupons will be there - even when you converted the cart to a purchase order. It's easily accessible
    via IOrderForm.CouponCodes until you explicitly remove it.

    Before being "applied", the promotions will be filtered by ICouponFilter. The default implemen-
    tation of ICouponFilter guarantees that: If no coupon was supplied, then all promotions which
    require coupons will be filtered out. If one or more coupons were supplied, then all promotions
    which coupons does not match the supplied coupons will be filtered. This will limit the number of
    promotions which needs to be applied - and help improving performance.

    ICouponFilter is a simple interface with only one method:

1 public interface ICouponFilter

2{

3     /// <summary>

4     /// Filters promotions by supplied coupon codes.

5     /// </summary>

6     /// <param name="filterContext">The filter context.</param>

7     /// <param name="couponCodes">The coupon codes added to the current order form.<\

8 /param>

9     /// <returns>

10    PromotionFilterContext Filter(PromotionFilterContext filterContext, IEnumerable<\

11 string> couponCodes);

12 }

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          267

The default implementation works with case when each promotion can only have one coupon. In
case you have multiple coupon codes per promotion, you will have to implement ICouponFilter
yourself.

Promotions exclusion

As we learned in previous section, in old promotion system, you only have three options for
exclusion: A promotion might exclude nothing, exclude all promotions in same group, or exclude
everything. This works to some extend, but it's not flexible enough. The new promotion system
provides much more powerful exclusion mechanism, to allow you quickly set the exclusion
combination between promotions.

Programmatically, the exclusion settings for a promotion can be set by ExcludedItems, which is a
collection of ContentReferences you want to be exclusive with the promotion. - If it is CampaignRoot,
then the promotion will exclude all other promotions. - If it is a SalesCampaign, then the promotion
will exclude all promotions in that campaign.

                 The ExcludedItems has been proved to a bad name and caused confusions among devel-
                 opers. Many people thinks it means for the items (Product/SKU) which cannot apply this
                 promotion. There was discussion about renaming it, but that is too risky, so we will have
                 to stick with it for now. Just keep in mind that you can only add a ContentReference of a
                 PromotionData, a SalesCampaign or CampaignRoot to ExcludedItems, not ContentReference
                 to catalog content.

    It's worth noting that, if you are using Commerce 9.24.1 and before, this is the way you should do
    it:

1 promotion.ExcludedItems = new List<ContentReference>() { otherPromotion.ContentLink,\
2 summerCampaign.ContentLink };

    If you are using Commerce 10, or later (which means you are using CMS 10 or later), you should be
    able to use this

1 //Exclude other promotion.
2 promotion.ExcludedItems.Add(otherPromotion.ContentLink);
3 //Excluded promotions in summer campaign.
4 promotion.ExcludedItems.Add(summerCampaign.ContentLink);

    The exclusion works both way. If you have two promotions, A and B, then adding B to A.ExcludedItems
    has same meaning as adding A to B.ExcludedItems. The order of exclusion, however, comes in pair
    with priority, it's basically the same rule as the old promotion system: a promotion with higher
    priority will exclude the promotions with lower priorities, not the way around.

                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                       268

What if A and B have same priority, and exclude each other, which will be applied and
which will be excluded? We don't know, and this is one case that you want to avoid - when
you set the exclusion, always make sure to set the correct order of priority.

As I said, the exclusion setting in new promotion system is very flexible:

                                               You can exclude anything and everything

By default, the exclusion level is Order, which means if a promotion is already applied to the cart,
then any promotions which are excluded by it, or exclude it (depends on the settings, as above) will
not be evaluated. That is sometimes not flexible enough. Here's an example: You have a specific
shoes which you want to give 30% off. You also want to give all the shoes 10%. If a customer buy
both the special shoes, and a normal shoes, you want them to get both discounts, however, you don't
want the special shoes to get that 10% off, so 30% off the special shoes and 10% off the normal shoes.

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                                          269

   Commerce 11.3 comes with the new option of having exclusion level at Unit level. It can be a bit
   more complicated than Order level. What would happen if the special shoes have the limitation of 1
   per customer, and the customer buys two pairs? They should at least get the 10% off on the second
   pair, right? That's the behavior of the default implementation (you can of course change it, we might
   get into it later). That's also why the exclusion level is called Unit, not LineItem.

   To turn on the Unit exclusion level, add this setting to appSettings:

1 <add key="PromotionExclusionLevel" value="Unit" />

    It's important to turn this on in both your front-end site, and Commerce Manager. Otherwise
    you might see some weird behaviors when the merchandisers manage the orders.

   Items exclusion

   In above section we talked about excluding specific promotions from be ing applied. How's about
   excluding all promotions for a specific item. Some items, such as gift cards, can be considered as
   money and should not be discounted in any situations (otherwise some sneaky customers might find
   a loophole to eventually get something for free, you don't want that, do you?). In the old promotion
   system, this is simply impossible - you don't have anyway to do that. That's why the new promotion
   system is superior, at this time of writing, a new feature is just available - allows you to decide which
   items should be hidden from the promotion system, entirely.

   This can be done via IEntryFilter, a simple interface which filters out entry codes which should
   not be checked by promotion engine:

1 public interface IEntryFilter

2{

3   /// <summary>

4   /// Filters out entry codes which should be excluded by the promotion engine.

5   /// </summary>

6   /// <param name="entryCodes">The entry codes to be filtered.</param>

7   IEnumerable<string> Filter(IEnumerable<string> entryCodes);

8}

   You can of course implement your own implementation, however, the default implementation is
   already very extensible and easy to use - with EntryFilterSettings you can practically exclude
   everything you want, both programmatically and manually. EntryFilterSettings has several
   methods, but the most important one is AddFilter<T>(Predicate<T> filter) - which allow you to
   specify which criteria to filter out entries. Here is what you can do in an IInitializationModule:

                                         Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system  270

 1 var filterSettings = context.Locate.Advanced.GetInstance<EntryFilterSettings>();
 2 //Clear all filter
 3 filterSettings.ClearFilters();
 4
 5 //Add filter predicates for a whole content type.
 6 filterSettings.AddFilter<TypeThatShouldNeverBeIncluded>(x => false);
 7
 8 //Add filter predicates for property of strongly typed content types.
 9 filterSettings.AddFilter<IInterfaceWithShouldBeExcludedProperty>(x => !x.ShouldBeExc\
10 luded);
11
12 //Add filter predicates for property of non strongly typed content types (properties\
13 which are created by metafields)
14 filterSettings.AddFilter<EntryContentBase>(x => !(bool)(x["ShouldBeExcludedMetaField\
15 "] ?? false));
16
17 //Add filter predicates for specific entries
18 var ExcludingCodes = new string[] { "SKU-36127195", "SKU-39850363", "SKU-39101253" };
19 filterSettings.AddFilter<EntryContentBase>(x => !ExcludingCodes.Contains(x.Code));

     Those are pretty "programmatically" - so how about "manually"? In the above example, you can
     have a predicate so if an entry has a property ("Excluded by promotion engine", for example) set to
     true, then it should be excluded by the promotion engine. When editors edit the entries, they can
     manually set that value in specific entries to be true. And voilà!

     Campaign and promotion events

     It's frequently asked on Episerver World forums - how to listen and react when a campaign or
     a promotion is changed (created/updated/deleted). And the answer is quite simple: campaign and
     promotion are just CMS-contents, modifications made to them will raise events in IContentEvents.
     So just hook your processing into the events of this interface (preferably in an initialization module).
     From there, you can have your extra processing, just like other CMS contents.

                       One thing that has been asked/requested is that when promotions and campaigns are
                       changed, then the affected entries will be reindexed so prices will be updated to latest values.
                       Of course you can listen to IContentEvents.PublishedContent to know when a campaign
                       or a promotion is published, but it is much more complicated than that. You can't know
                       when a scheduled campaign/promotion gets activated or expired.

Getting discounted prices
So far, the promotion engine only works on orders. But a common case is to display the discounted
prices of products when a customer is browsing the site - aka visiting the product listing page or

                                                 Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          271

    checking the search result. This is of course important - a product at 100$ is surely not as attractive
    as itself, with 20$ discount so it only costs 80$. How?

    In the old promotion system, you have StoreHelper.GetDiscountPrice, which take an Entry and
    some other parameters, to do the (almost) same thing. (StoreHelper is a class in Mediachase.Commerce.Website,
    which is an open source project. You can download it and have fun!). So the most common way to
    get discounts for product listing page is to load the Entry instance for each entry, and call to that
    method to get the discounted price. It's undoubtedly slow. What's about the new promotion system?

    It comes with some extension methods for IPromotionEngine:

1   public static IEnumerable<DiscountedEntry> GetDiscountPrices(this IPromotionEngi\

2 ne promotionEngine,

3   ContentReference entryLink,

4   IMarket market);

5

6   public static IEnumerable<DiscountedEntry> GetDiscountPrices(this IPromotionEngi\

7 ne promotionEngine,

8   ContentReference entryLink,

9   IMarket market,

10  Currency currency);

11

12  public static IEnumerable<DiscountedEntry> GetDiscountPrices(this IPromotionEngi\

13 ne promotionEngine,

14  IEnumerable<ContentReference> entryLinks,

15  IMarket market,

16  Currency marketCurrency);

    These methods will return a list of DiscountedEntry, which has the information about the entry (the
    entryLink, actually), the default price, the discounted price and the promotion associated with it. It
    can be called with one or multiple ContentReference - a big plus compared to the old promotion.
    ContentReference is also faster, easier to get and use less memory than Entry.

    How is it done internally? The promotion system will create an in-memory order, which contains
    that entry (with quantity = 1, in case of multiple entries, each will be evaluated individually). It then
    try to evaluate the promotions on that order. Of course, any promotions which require coupon, or
    at least 2 items to be fulfilled, will not be applied.

    Disable built-in promotions

    At this time of writing, Episerver Commerce already has more than 10 built-in promotion types -
    they are quite enough to cover almost every case of promotions. However, sometimes you don't
    want to use those built-in types, but your custom types, and you want to hide the built-in ones so
    to make it's easier for editors to work with promotions. How?

                                          Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          272

   If we recall from the beginning of this chapter, promotion types are just content types - so if you
   want to disable a specific promotion, you can just set the availability of the corresponding content
   type to false - it'll be hidden.

1 var type = typeof(SpendAmountGetDiscount);

2 var contentType = ServiceLocator.Current.GetInstance<IContentTypeRepository>.Load(ty\

3 pe).CreateWritableClone() as ContentType;

4 if (contentType.IsAvailable)

5{

6   contentType.IsAvailable = available;

7   _contentTypeRepository.Save(contentType);

8}

   Luckily, you won't have to write such code yourself. Commerce 9.20 comes with new PromotionTypeHandler
   which allows you to disable/enable promotion types with ease:

1 public class PromotionTypeHandler

2{

3   public void DisablePromotions(IEnumerable<Type> promotionTypes);

4   public void EnablePromotions(IEnumerable<Type> promotionTypes);

5   public void DisableBuiltinPromotions();

6   public IEnumerable<Type> GetAllPromotionTypes();

7}

   So let's say we want to disable all of the shipping promotion types - because you are selling digital
   content and it does not really make senses to have such promotions - for example. So we should add
   this to an initialization module:

1 private void DisablePromotionTypes(InitializationEngine context)

2{

3   var promotionTypeHandler = context.Locate.Advanced.GetInstance<PromotionTypeHand\

4 ler>();

5   var promotionTypes = promotionTypeHandler.GetAllPromotionTypes();

6   var shippingPromotionTypes = promotionTypes.Where(t => t.IsSubclassOf(typeof(Shi\

7 ppingPromotion)));

8   promotionTypeHandler.DisablePromotions(shippingPromotionTypes);

9}

                                         Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          273

                                 And then the shipping promotion types won't bother you again.

New promotion system in Commerce Manager

The new promotion system basically works in Commerce Manager, what's the point of having
promotions that cannot be applied when you manage the orders? However, due to nature of
Commerce Manager and strategy from Episerver, there are extra steps needed to make it work:

   · The VNextFeature flag needs to be enabled as Commerce Manager relies on the workflows
      for discounts calculation, either by code of by ecf.app.config. (If your site was created newly
      from 9.19 or later, this is no longer needed. However if you use an older version, or you upgrade
      from an older version, this must be enabled manually.)

   · Your promotion content types must be present in Commerce Manager bin folder. For that
      reason, you should place all of your promotion content types in one project (along with your
      shipment gateways and payment providers), to make it easier to manage.

In 9.19, all of known issues have been addressed and some new small UI tunings have been added
to Commerce Manager. The Marketing section is also disabled if the VNextFeature flag is enabled:

                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 13: The new promotion system                                          274

                                      Goodbye, old promotion system!

                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Part 7: Business Foundation

This chapter is dedicated to one of the most advance and in some ways, most mysterious features in
Episerver Commerce - Business Foundation. What is it, how does it work, how can it be extended.
Chapter 14: Business Foundation
overview

If you're new to Episerver Commerce, Business Foundation is a quite hidden feature. In a standard
Commerce installation, the only place where you can find BF is in Administration/System Settings
of Commerce Manager.

                                  Configure business foundation objects in Commerce Manager

Here you can see the list of predefined business objects. Some of them are obsolete and are almost
unused anywhere (FolderEntity, FolderElementEntity for example). But you'll likely to work with
Address, Contact, ContactNote and CreditCard. Like the MetaClass system for catalog and order,
you can define your own business object.
    Chapter 14: Business Foundation overview                                         277

                                             Create new Business Object in Commerce Manager

    And you can import and export the settings. (Unlike the catalog system, where you must export the
    classes with the data, here you can export and import only the definition of classes.)
    Business Objects are built around EntityObject, which itself is a simple class:

1 public class EntityObject

2{

3     public EntityObject(string metaClassName);

4

5     public EntityObject(string metaClassName, PrimaryKeyId primaryKeyId);

6

7     public string MetaClassName { get; set;}

8

9     public PrimaryKeyId? PrimaryKeyId { get; set;}

10

11    public EntityObjectPropertyCollection Properties { get; set;}

12

13    public object this[string name] { get; set;}

14 }

    Each EntityObject is identified by its PrimaryKeyId and MetaClassName. PrimaryKeyId is yet
    another struct to identify an object - it can take an int or a Guid.

      If you recall from Part 1 - there are two classes named MetaClass. The one in
      Mediachase.BusinessFoundation.Data.Business is the one we need right now.

    EntityObject is just the base class. Every business object extends it to add necessary fields and
    relations. However, all of are still managed by BusinessManager - the heart of Business Foundation.
    BusinessManager provides plenty of CRUD - Create, Read (both single object and list), Update and
    Delete - operations to work with EntityObject.

    Here's an example to create an Organization, which of course, inherits from EntityObject.
    Organization is one of Business Objects available out-of-box.

                             Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
   Chapter 14: Business Foundation overview                                                       278

1 var organization = new Organization();
2 organization.Name = "Umbrella Corp";
3 organization.OrganizationType = "Evil Corporation";
4 var organizationId = BusinessManager.Create(organization);

    In this example, we are using BusinessManager.Create(EntityObject object) to create a new
    EntityObject, and return the PrimaryKeyId of the newly created object.
    And then we can load it back, and edit a field, then save:

1 var organization = BusinessManager.Load("Organization", organizationId);
2 organization.OrganizationType = "Defunded Evil Corporation";
3 BusinessManager.Update(organization);

    "Organization" is the name of the MetaClass. We can also use List which returns the list of objects:

1 public static EntityObject[] List(string metaClassName, FilterElement[] filters)
2 public static EntityObject[] List(string metaClassName, FilterElement[] filters, Sor\
3 tingElement[] sorting)
4 public static EntityObject[] List(string metaClassName, SortingElement[] sorting)
5 public static EntityObject[] List(string metaClassName, FilterElement[] filters, Sor\
6 tingElement[] sorting, int? start, int? count)

    FilterElement is a simple class which helps you to filter the list based on some conditions:

1 public class FilterElement

2{

3   public virtual string Source {get; set;}

4   public virtual FilterElementType Type {get; set;}

5   public virtual object Value {get; set;}

6   public bool ValueIsTemplate { get; set;}

7}

   Basically, one FilterElement means we can filter business objects which has Source field which
   match (or not match) Value. Value can be a template string. Type can be one of those values: Equal,
   NotEqual, Less, LessOrEqual,...

   And finally, we can delete it:

1 BusinessManager.Update("Organization", organizationId);

    Internally, all operations from BusinessManager are built around this single, simple method:

                              Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 14: Business Foundation overview                           279

1 Response Execute(Request request)

    Request is a base class of all others "request" classes, it contains the EntityObject to be worked on,
    and the method of that work

1 public class Request

2{

3     public EntityObject Target {get; set;}

4     public string Method {get; set;}

5}

    The request classes are only different in RequestMethod property:

1 public static class RequestMethod

2{

3     public const string InitializeEntity = "InitializeEntity";

4

5     public const string Create = "Create";

6     public const string Update = "Update";

7     public const string Delete = "Delete";

8     public const string Load = "Load";

9     public const string List = "List";

10

11    public const string Import = "Import";

12    public const string InitializeMappingDocument = "InitializeMappingDocument";

13 }

    So you can guess, a DeleteRequest is a Request with RequestMethod.Delete - and so on and so
    forth.

    Simple, right?

    Number of objects

    If you have a fairly big number of objects in Business Foundation, you might realize that your
    BusinessManager.List call only returns 1000 objects, no matter how many objects you actually
    have.

    That is actually a known behavior (but not very well known, for what it's worth.). Business
    Foundation only loads first 1000 objects, for history reasons (mostly performance). Luckily for us,
    that can be changed by a setting in Configs\baf.data.metaobject.config

                        Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 14: Business Foundation overview  280

1 <?xml version="1.0"?>
2 <metaObject maxListObject="1000">
3 </metaObject>

    By changing maxListObject you can change the maximum number of objects that BusinessManager.List
    returns.

                     Note that changing that number to a very big value will have bad performance implication,
                     so only change it when you are absolutely sure about what you are doing.

Extending Business Objects

Business Foundation is all about extending objects so they can fit your needs. How? If you recall
about MetaDataPlus metadata system, then you know about the MetaClass(es) and MetaField(s), and
the relations between them. With Business Foundation, every thing is almost the same, we also have
MetaClass and MetaField, but instead of Mediachase.MetaDataPlus, we use Mediachase.BusinessFoundation.Data
here. And instead of MetaObject for each instance, we have EntityObject as we talked in previous
section.

                                                       Adding new MetaField to a MetaClass

    Business Object can be extended by Business Foundation section in Commerce Manager, but of
    course it can also be extended by code. The legacy way to extend Business Object is to use IPlugin
    interface. it's a quite simple one with only one method:

1 void Execute(BusinessContext context);

    Whenever BusinessManager.Execute(Request request) is called, it will try to load all classes which
    implement IPlugin and run them (!). So we will have to use some mechanism to make sure that the
    module was not executed, and that check should be really fast. But that was pre-Episerver era, and
    for now we should use IInitializationModule (or even better, Migration steps) to do such task. We
    then only need to check for that module once on startup.

                                                      Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
    Chapter 14: Business Foundation overview                            281

    This is the check to see if we should continue creating the metaclass (named "CreditCard". It's simple
    as that, every MetaClass in Business Foundation is available in DataContext.Current.MetaModel.MetaClasses.

1 if (DataContext.Current.MetaModel.MetaClasses["CreditCard"] == null)

2{

3        return;

4}

    If there was no "CreditCart" MetaClass, let's go ahead and add it:

1 MetaClassManager manager = DataContext.Current.MetaModel;

    using (MetaClassManagerEditScope scope = manager.BeginEdit(MetaClassManagerEditScope.SystemOwner,
    AccessLevel.System))

1{

2        ModuleManifest changeTrackingManifest = ChangeTrackingManager.CreateModuleManifest(\

3 );

4        MetaClass mc = manager.CreateMetaClass("CreditCard", "Credit Card", "CreditCards", \

5 "cls_CreditCard", PrimaryKeyIdValueType.Guid);

6

7        ModuleManager.Activate(mc, changeTrackingManifest);

8        using (MetaFieldBuilder builder = new MetaFieldBuilder(mc))

9        {

10                mc.TitleFieldName = "CreditCardNumber";

11                builder.CreateText("CreditCardNumber", "Credit Card Number", false, 512, false);

12                builder.CreateEnumField("CardType", "Credit Card Type", "CreditCardType", false, s\

13 tring.Empty, true);

14                builder.CreateText("LastFourDigits", "Last Four Digits", false, 4, false);

15                builder.CreateText("SecurityCode", "Security Code", true, 255, false);

16                builder.CreateInteger("ExpirationYear", "Expiration Year", true, null);

17                builder.CreateInteger("ExpirationMonth", "Expiration Month", true, null);

18                mc.Fields[MetaClassManager.GetPrimaryKeyName(mc.Name)].FriendlyName = "Primary Key\

19 id";

20                builder.SaveChanges();

21       }

22       scope.SaveChanges();

23 }

    We are creating CreditCard class using DataContext.Current.MetaModel.CreateMetaClass. (Note
    that this class is available by default in Commerce installation, we are using it here for example).
    Objects in this class will be saved to cls_CreditCard table in your database, with a primary key of
    type Guid.

                               Creating powerful eCommerce solutions.
Chapter 14: Business Foundation overview                                                       282

Of course you can choose the name of table as you like, but it's conventionally set to "cls_"
prefix - as a shorthand of "class".

     Next step is to add fields to the class - MetaFieldBuilder supports all the types available to
     MetaField:

 2 public MetaField CreateDateTime(string name, string friendlyName, bool isNullable, b\
 3 ool useUserTimeZone)

14 able, bool? isChecked, string trueLabelText, string falseLabelText)
